# Technical PRD — Voice AI Mock Case Interview (Feature 1, Build Spec)

**Companion to:** `prd-voice-ai-mock-case.md` (product PRD — source of *what* and *why*)
**This document:** the engineering build spec for an AI coding agent (Claude Code). It pins down stack, data model, API surface, orchestration logic, repo structure, and build order.
**Status:** Draft v1, updated through interviewer-behavior v4.3 (2026-09-29) · **Target:** MVP validation build · **Scale:** tens of pilot users (consulting clubs), bursty seasonal concurrency.

> Read this alongside the product PRD. Where the product PRD states a requirement (FR-N), this doc says how to build it. The product PRD wins on intent; this doc wins on implementation detail. If they conflict, flag it — don't silently pick one.

> **Companion normative docs** (added as the build matured; this PRD points to them rather than duplicating):
> - `docs/interviewer-behavior.md` — the authored interviewer conduct spec (Rules 1–19, incl. the conduct/wellbeing track; currently v4.3, with an implementation-status register in Part V). Governs §5.4.
> - `docs/scoring-qa.md` — judge, report-verification pipeline, and scoring-attribution requirements (implemented/pending marked). Governs §6.
> - `docs/case-authoring.md` — case-content authoring + the ledger-consistency QA gate. Governs §10.

---

## 0. The one decision that shapes the whole build

**Build the text case end-to-end before any voice infrastructure exists.** The entire case state machine, data-gating, interviewer agent, scoring engine, and feedback report MUST run and be testable over **typed input** with zero STT/TTS/WebRTC in the stack. Voice (M2) is a transport layer that wraps the proven text core — not a prerequisite.

Concretely: the orchestrator's interface to the candidate is an abstract `CandidateChannel` (sends interviewer text, receives candidate text + control signals). The text build implements it with an HTTP/WebSocket text channel. The voice build implements the *same interface* with the LiveKit voice pipeline. **Nothing in the orchestrator, agent, or scoring code may import a voice library.**

This is the single most important constraint in the document. It de-risks the expensive work (the feedback engine — "the rubric is the product") before the realism work (voice).

---

## 1. Stack

| Layer | Choice | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Framework | **Next.js (App Router)** | Server actions for mutations; route handlers for streaming/voice endpoints. |
| Hosting | **Vercel** | Note: long-lived voice sessions don't belong on Vercel serverless — see §8.4. |
| DB + Auth | **Supabase** (Postgres + Supabase Auth) | Email + **club code** gating. RLS on all candidate-owned tables. |
| ORM / DB access | Supabase client; **`drizzle`** for typed schema + migrations (agent's choice if it prefers Supabase migrations directly — flag the decision). |
| Styling | Tailwind + a component lib of the agent's choice (shadcn/ui acceptable). Keep mid-case UI minimal (§10 product PRD). |
| Live interviewer LLM | **Claude Sonnet 5.5** (`claude-sonnet-5-5`), thinking off (`between_tools`), structured JSON turn, streamed | Since 12 Oct (batch 20: Sonnet 20.7 D1–D9 faults per 100 turns vs Haiku 5.5's 32.7). Server-side refusal fallback on (`fallbacks: "default"`); request settings follow the model via `interviewerRequestOptions`. Haiku 5.5 (thinking disabled, effort medium, no fallback — it has none) stays selectable: `INTERVIEWER_PROVIDER=anthropic-haiku55-none-medium`. Evaluations name an explicit config (`INTERVIEWER_EVAL_CONFIG` / `persona-batch --interviewer=`): `sonnet55-baseline`, `haiku55-thinking-off` or `haiku55-effort-low`; a run answered by another model fails. Since 6 Oct the model returns one JSON turn — `{say, move, requests, exhibit, rescue_item, question}` — and makes no data, phase or ending decisions (§5.3). Swappable behind the `InterviewerModel` interface; ids in `lib/models.ts`. Screened and not adopted (7–8 Oct; adapters kept behind switches): Cerebras gpt-oss-120b, GPT-6 Luna and Sol, GPT-6.1 Sol, Qwen 3.8 27B, Gemini 3.8 Flash — results in §16 (15 Oct, body trim). History: Haiku 4.5 → Opus 4.8 (July 2026, missed live math errors) → Sonnet 5.5 (Oct, speed and cost) → Haiku 5.5 (11 Oct, latency) → Sonnet 5.5 (12 Oct, quality); the deterministic math backstops now carry most of the math-catching load — watch batch results for regressions. |
| Scoring/judge LLM | **Claude Opus 5.5** (`claude-opus-5-5`), adaptive thinking, effort `high` | Runs once at case end; latency-insensitive; this is the product. Three Opus passes: judge, claim verifier, dimension reconciliation (§6). Oct 2026: moved from Opus 4.8. Server-side refusal fallback on. |
| Background LLM passes | Per role in `lib/models.ts` (`BACKGROUND_MODEL_ID`): **Claude Haiku 5.5** (`claude-haiku-5-5`, thinking disabled, effort medium) for the pressure-test judges and the C5 distress check since 8 Oct; **Claude Haiku 4.5** for coverage, the data-request classifier and the hint check | **Haiku 5.5 migration (8 Oct; spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-08-haiku-5-5-background-migration.md`):** each role switched only after a targeted regression check on fixed saved inputs. Judges: fewer false unlocks than 4.5. Distress: same fires on 271 real messages. Hint check failed (stays 4.5). Data-request: unparseable without a schema; with an enforced schema it parses but over-maps asks to ledger items (stays 4.5). Coverage: same end gate, different mid-case steer (stays 4.5). Live coverage agent and Rule 11 data-request classifier run after the response (`after()`). In parallel with every interviewer call: the C5 distress check (v4.6) — it adds latency only when it outlasts the model's first sentence (batch 11: verdict at 0.7–0.95s, never waited on). Same-turn data-request detection was removed from model turns on 6 Oct (the model declares the requests; it fed only a log-only audit). Synchronous: recommendation-ask turns classify the current message (~1s), and turns where a hint's delivery rests on a question run the hint check — count both against the M2 latency budget. |
| STT | **Deepgram Flux** (`flux-general-en`, streaming, model end-of-turn) | M0 Phase A (6–7 Oct): Flux over Nova-3 — Nova-3's default 300ms endpointing ended the turn mid-answer on 7/10 long answers, Flux on 3/41 (all at thinking pauses). Behind `STTProvider` (`lib/voice/deepgram.ts`, both adapters). STT mishears case names ("Brew & Bean" → "brewing bean") — keyterms per case are open. Settings: end-of-turn 0.7 / eager 0.5; a 7 Oct synthetic sweep found no setting both faster and safer — human recordings decide any change (§8.5). |
| TTS | **Cartesia** (Sonic 3.6 since 18 Oct — 3.5 before — voice 533b2990 by default, `CARTESIA_VOICE_ID` overrides; Lorenzo's pick by ear; one WebSocket context per interviewer turn) | M0 Phase A: first audio ~125–155ms after a segment. Behind `TTSProvider` (`lib/voice/cartesia.ts`). Free tier exhausted during the spike; the pilot needs Pro (commercial use). Deepgram Aura used for the listening demo (`scripts/voice-demo.ts`) — per-segment REST calls reset the voice between sentences; Cartesia's per-turn context avoids that. |
| Voice transport / orchestration | **LiveKit Agents** | WebRTC, VAD/endpointing, barge-in. **Caveat:** data-gating lives in OUR orchestrator, not LiveKit's loop — see §8. Not yet tested: M0 Phase B (transport, mic, barge-in) not started. |

**Supabase keys (non-negotiable):**
- Client-side: `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` (publishable key).
- Server-only: `SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY` — never behind `NEXT_PUBLIC_` or any client-exposed var.
- Use the new publishable/secret keys, **not** legacy anon/service_role JWTs.

**All vendor choices marked spike-pending are swappable behind interfaces.** The M0 spike validates latency on the cascade before committing. If the spike fails the ≤1.5s gate, the interface boundary is where you swap providers — no orchestrator rewrite.

---

## 2. System architecture

```
┌─────────────┐     CandidateChannel (abstract)      ┌──────────────────┐
│  Candidate  │◄────────────────────────────────────►│   Orchestrator   │
│  (browser)  │   text channel  OR  voice pipeline    │  (session state, │
└─────────────┘                                        │  data ledger,    │
                                                       │  phase logic,    │
   TEXT BUILD (M1):  WebSocket text channel            │  action exec)    │
   VOICE BUILD (M2): LiveKit ⟷ Deepgram/Cartesia       └────────┬─────────┘
                                                                │
                                                    per-turn    │  declarations
                                                    prompt      │  + words
                                                                ▼
                                                       ┌──────────────────┐
                                                       │ Interviewer LLM  │
                                                       │  (Sonnet 5.5,    │
                                                       │   JSON turn)     │
                                                       └──────────────────┘

   case end ──► Scoring service (Opus 5.5 judge/verifier/reconciliation + deterministic checks) ──► Report
```

**Core principle:** the orchestrator is the source of truth and the only thing that touches the data ledger. The LLM never sees a number it isn't allowed to reveal yet (see §5). The candidate channel is swappable; the orchestrator is not.

---

## 3. Repository structure (proposed — agent may refine, flag changes)

```
/app                      Next.js App Router
  /(marketing)            landing, club-code entry
  /case/[sessionId]       live case UI (text channel for M1, voice for M2)
  /case/[sessionId]/report  feedback report
  /api
    /session              create/advance/end session (route handlers)
    /channel              text/voice channel endpoints
/lib
  /orchestrator           session state machine, phase logic, action executor
    state-machine.ts      INTRO→…→SCORING transitions
    data-ledger.ts        reveal gating; the ONLY module that discloses numbers
    channel.ts            CandidateChannel abstract interface
    session-runner.ts     one candidate turn end to end (runTurn): reads → plan → stream → settle → commit
    plan-turn.ts          Plan stage: every pre-model decision, the turn kind (§5.2), no writes
    stream-turn.ts        Stream stage: delivers say sentences + code's data line as the model writes (vetoReason)
    speculation.ts, speculative-turn.ts   speculative turn on Flux's eager signal — DraftGate, plan fingerprint, startDraft. IMPLEMENTED, DISABLED: no live caller (§8.2)
    prompt-context.ts     the interviewer's per-turn prompt context from a plan (shared by the runner and the speculation fingerprint)
    pressure-test.ts      Rule 7 pressure-test state (not_asked/awaiting/satisfied/waived), probe detection, the Haiku answer judge, gate scope, bounded fallback questions (8 Oct); v3 reply bound, re-ask / narrowed probe / waiver, probe-as-heard for the judge (10 Oct)
    request-signal.ts     guard B's explicit-ask detector (phrasing; precision-tuned) and the sentences it keeps for request_signal events; addressedAskSentences / matchAskDetections — which classifier detections a code-written turn answers (9 Oct)
    settle-turn.ts        Settle stage: composes the turn, books reveals, derives phase, one commit
    turn-record.ts        TurnRecorder — one evaluation record per turn (exact model requests, raw replies, state before, check verdicts, timed delivery, recorder errors); filled only when runTurn gets onTurnRecord (evaluation runs), production passes none (9 Oct)
    data-decisions.ts     Rule 11 decided in code from the model's declared requests; renders every data line
    turn-data.ts          one turn's data decisions (shared by Stream and Settle)
    progress.ts           phase and stage administration from declared moves (Rule 8, §5.2)
    data-requests.ts      Rule 11 data-request classifier (Haiku) + open-request / force-release helpers
    post-turn.ts          background passes after each turn (coverage agent, data-request audit)
    start-session.ts      session creation + deterministic opening turn
    numeric-provenance.ts provenance audit + enforcement (blocked sentences withheld, Rule 6)
    recompute.ts          live math check → probe/correct hint + Rule 14 attempt counter; flags of kind step / unit / arithmetic / pricing with the claim (quantity, units, base, timeframe); `checkCandidateMath` is the one flags-and-stamps computation the live turn and scoring share (13 Oct)
    arithmetic-check.ts   stated operations verified exactly: correct / incorrect / unclear (v4.10)
    unit-check.ts         nested-percentage conversions checked numerically against the case's shareBases (v4.10; the keyword detector is gone); a conversion is checked at the base the candidate states, and one right on that base with a share of another year is a period question, not a flag (15 Oct)
    math-checker.ts       background math checker (13 Oct): a model checks each candidate message with a quantitative claim against the full case; per-claim verdicts; its probe and pointer probe vetted for FR-4 in code; corrections written by code from its own computation
    math-checker-live.ts  the checker in the live turn (13 Oct; wired, not yet run live): started in Plan, never awaited, its verdict a session event read by later turns as a Rule 14 flag (stale / dedupe / expiry, one a turn, the close hold)
    math-talk.ts          reads whether an interviewer sentence doubts the candidate's math or asks for the working (v4.10)
    conduct.ts            C1–C5 conduct classifier (Rule 17) — regex floor; ambiguous C3 stems → "confirm" (v4.10)
    conduct-model.ts      Rule 17 model layer: the C3 backstop and tiebreaker, beside the interviewer call (v4.10)
    distress.ts           C5 model layer (Haiku, parallel with the interviewer call); after a declined offer, judges only a new or worse signal (v4.10)
    candidate-exit.ts     Rule 18A: stop requests, bare sign-offs, refusals to start, the reply to "do you want to stop?" (v4.10)
    session-end.ts, session-outcome.ts   the End button and the shared "report or not" rule (v4.10)
    stage-evidence.ts     the stage record: delivered + outcome per stage; owed stages before a normal end (Rule 9 v4.10)
    probe-guard.ts        doubt on the candidate's math only with a flag; explain probes once per figure (Rule 2 v4.5 → v4.10)
    assumption-guard.ts   withholds challenges to assumptions about requested data (Rule 11 v4.5)
    timeframe-check.ts    cross-period arithmetic in interviewer speech, log-only (Rule 6 v4.6; the candidate side is unit-check's period flag)
    hint-check.ts         model check that a question-based rung was really a hint (Rule 13)
    check-log.ts          per-turn decision log for every check (Part V v4.6)
    silence.ts, spoken-close.ts, stall.ts, pacing.ts   silence/pause, one-goodbye close, stall ladder, time budgets + grace ask
  /agent
    interviewer.ts        per-turn prompt assembly, catalog id resolver, the streamed turn
    prompts/              system prompt, anti-hallucination, anti-jailbreak; system-compact.ts — compact-prompt A/B arm, not on the live path (7 Oct)
    models/               InterviewerModel interface + Anthropic impl (Sonnet 5.5; ids in lib/models.ts); turn-schema.ts, turn-stream.ts; factory.ts picks the provider (INTERVIEWER_PROVIDER), cerebras.ts kept behind it, unused (7 Oct)
    opener.ts             Haiku-written opener — experiment, replay arm only, not on the live path (7 Oct)
  /scoring
    judge.ts              Opus rubric evaluation over full transcript
    deterministic.ts      math-tolerance checks, data-leak audit (NOT the LLM)
    report.ts             assembles the 8-dimension report + model answer
    score-session.ts      the full scoring pipeline (§6), shared by the score route and scripts
    transcript-artifacts.ts, evidence-audit.ts, verifier.ts, reconcile.ts   report verification (§6)
    data-coverage.ts      requested-vs-never-requested data split for the judge (Rule 11)
    math-spans.ts         source spans for math checks, shared by live recompute and scoring (Rules 2/3)
    interviewer-errors.ts interviewer-error + wellbeing marks from the event log (Rule 3, 17-C5)
    caveat-floor.ts       caveated dimensions: meets_bar only on remaining evidence, else not assessed (Rule 9 v4.10)
    challenge-marks.ts    delivered doubts → confirmed_interviewer_error / unsupported_challenge; claims code settled, for the judge (Rule 3 v4.10)
    stage-caps.ts         the stage record for the judge; a dimension whose stage never happened is rated on evidence or not assessed; partial reports (Rule 9 v4.10)
    answer-key-pass.ts    no missing-answer-key-idea weaknesses; stage faults under not-run caveats (Rule 3 v4.5)
    strong-gate.ts        "strong" only on an evidenced strong-anchor checklist; overall capped
  models.ts               every model id + the server-side fallback setting; background classifier ids by role + Haiku 5.5 request settings (backgroundRequest)
  llm-pricing.ts          one price table by exact model id (cache, Haiku 5.5 long-prompt tier, Cerebras qwen-3.8-27b for screening); unknown ids throw — never $0 (8 Oct)
  anthropic-client.ts, llm-meter.ts, llm-budget.ts   the one Anthropic client; its fetch meter checks a run budget before each call and records usage from the response (JSON and SSE, aborted streams estimated); LLM_BUDGET_USD / LLM_BUDGET_FILE (8 Oct); optional extra cap LLM_EXTRA_BUDGET_USD + LLM_EXTRA_BUDGET_FILE, checked and recorded beside the main one — a targeted check inside a batch (10 Oct)
  number-words.ts         spoken numbers → digits for the stall signal and math spans
  /eval                   persona-batch evaluation (spec docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-09-persona-batch-evaluation-design.md; dev only, never imported by app/):
                          turn-file.ts (<run>.turns.jsonl.gz: header, TurnRecords, end line), git-info.ts (commit + code fingerprint),
                          batch-manifest.ts (waves per batch, run numbering, freeze on code fingerprint + model ids + bands/severity hashes, versions, reruns; batch 18 frozen; per-run requested/returned interviewer model),
                          model-guard.ts (the declared evaluation interviewer; requested vs returned model per call; unpriced/unknown/mismatch fails the run) (v4.10),
                          wave-commands.ts, scorecard.ts (one row per interview: completion, stages, loops, code flags, opportunities, latency, cost, ratings, evidence),
                          prescreen.ts (Opus fault proposals, verbatim-quote check), review-sheet.ts + headline.ts (human confirmation, batch headline), bands.ts (reports vs persona bands)
  /voice                  STTProvider/TTSProvider (types.ts), Deepgram Flux/Nova-3 (deepgram.ts), Cartesia (cartesia.ts), segment → speech (speak.ts), instant acknowledgment (acknowledge.ts; the thinking filler was removed 7 Oct), playback scheduler (playback.ts), end-of-turn clip variants + signal scoring (endpoint.ts), PCM helpers (pcm.ts) — M0 Phase A; LiveKit glue is Phase B
  /cases                  case JSON loader + schema validation (zod)
/cases                    human-authored case content (JSON, version-controlled)
/db                       drizzle schema + migrations
/tests                    QA harness (incl. 50-case hallucination run); tests/replay/ re-runs the deterministic checks over saved persona runs (free; skips without them)
/scripts                  live-run.ts — end-to-end run with a simulated candidate against real models + DB (dev only; --finish re-scores a saved session);
                          regrade.ts — dry-run re-grade of stored sessions; eval-distress.ts — C5 model-layer eval (all cost money);
                          voice and latency experiments: voice-latency.ts, voice-demo.ts, endpoint-sweep.ts, replay-output-format.ts (cost money);
                          useful-latency.ts, playback-sim.ts (free — read saved runs);
                          evaluation: live-run --script / --seed / --seed-through / --max-turns (scripted and saved-prefix replays, no Opus),
                          guard-check.ts, review-dump.ts, request-signal-check.ts, plan-inspect.ts (free — read saved runs);
                          eval-probe-judge.ts, eval-background.ts (per-role Haiku 5.5 regression checks on fixed saved inputs; --dry estimates cost free);
                          every paid script requires LLM_BUDGET_USD (lib/llm-budget.ts);
                          persona batch (9 Oct): persona-batch.ts — one wave of ≤10 personas in parallel (cost money; --dry free; needs --env-file=.env.local);
                          batch-scorecard.ts, batch-review-sheet.ts (--write/--apply), batch-bands.ts (free); batch-prescreen.ts (Opus, cost money, resumable)
/evals                    frozen batch-evaluation inputs: severity.md (fault severity definitions), persona-bands.json (expected rating per persona × dimension)
```

The `/voice` directory must not be imported by `/lib/orchestrator`, `/lib/agent`, or `/lib/scoring`. Enforce with a lint rule (e.g. `eslint-plugin-boundaries`) so M1 stays voice-free by construction.

## 4. Data model (Supabase / Postgres)

RLS on every candidate-owned row: a candidate reads/writes only their own sessions. Cases and exhibits are readable by authenticated users; never expose `data_ledger` answer values or `*_key` fields to the client (see §7 — these stay server-side).

```
users                  (Supabase Auth)
  id, email, club_code, created_at

cases                  -- human-authored, version-controlled in /cases, synced to DB
  id (text, e.g. "prof-001"), title, firm_style, difficulty,
  prompt, content_jsonb (full case schema), version, active

sessions
  id (uuid), user_id (fk), case_id (fk),
  phase (enum), elapsed_ms, phase_started_at,
  status (active|completed|abandoned|terminated), abandon_phase,
  started_at, completed_at,
  coverage_jsonb         -- per-dimension live coverage (0-100 evidence, NOT
                         --   quality) from the background coverage agent;
                         --   steers the interviewer + gates an early close
  flags_jsonb            -- orchestrator run-state. Booleans: stalled, ran_long,
                         --   asked_repeat, off_topic_count, advanced_last_turn
                         --   (gates the no-back-to-back behavior shift),
                         --   time_warning_fired, load_shed_logged,
                         --   grace_ask_fired (Rule 12 time-up ask, v4.3).
                         -- Sub-objects: stall{} (stall-ladder state, Rule 13),
                         --   conduct{ warnings, distress_offered,
                         --   distress_offered_at_ms (C5 clock pause), category }
                         --   (Rule 17), silence{} (check-in / technical-pause
                         --   state, Rules 13/16/19), recompute_attempts{ step_id:
                         --   n } (Rule 14 attempt counter, v4.3).
                         -- (pushback_done is vestigial — the "push back once"
                         --   nag was removed; general rigor rules cover it.)

session_turns
  id, session_id (fk), turn_index, role (interviewer|candidate),
  text, timestamp_ms, latency_ms (nullable, voice),
  transcript_confidence (nullable, STT)

revealed_data
  id, session_id (fk), ledger_item_id, revealed_at_ms
  -- the data-disclosure trail; supports scoring + prevents re-ask confusion

exhibits_shown
  id, session_id (fk), exhibit_id, shown_at_ms

session_events         -- typed log; three categories share one table
  id, session_id (fk), category (intervention|conduct|data_request), subtype,
  turn_index, phase, payload_jsonb, created_at
  -- 'intervention': stall-ladder assists (restate_anchor|narrow_frame|
  --    directive_rescue), synthesis_unresolved, load_shed — SCORING inputs
  --    ("assisted ≠ covered" and time-pressure coverage caveats); also
  --    silence_check_in, technical_pause(_expired), session_resumed,
  --    recompute_flag (payload: step, candidate value, span, attempt — v4.3),
  --    grace_ask (v4.3). Only ladder rungs count as assists.
  -- 'conduct': C1–C5 conduct events (Rule 17), plus C2_excluded (quoted /
  --    reported / generic-you speech, logged not warned — v4.3). Internal
  --    only, NEVER surfaced to client/report (Rule 18, FERPA). Scoring reads
  --    'conduct' for ONE purpose (v4.3, interviewer-error marking): to EXCLUDE
  --    C2-warning and C5 exchanges from candidate evidence. It never feeds a
  --    penalty, and the judge is told not to mention either in the report.
  -- 'data_request': Rule 11 audit — one row per candidate data request
  --    (subtype release|refuse|defer|clarify|none; payload what, ledgerItemIds,
  --    revealedByNow) plus a 'classified' marker per checked exchange, so
  --    scoring can backfill exchanges the background pass missed.

scores
  id, session_id (fk),
  -- 8 rubric dimensions, each: <dim>_rating (enum; NULL when the dimension
  --   is "not assessed", Rule 9 v4.3) + <dim>_evidence_jsonb (legacy):
  --   structure, quantitative, data_exhibit, judgment, creativity,
  --   synthesis, communication, pushback
  overall_rating (enum), top_fix (text),
  rubric_jsonb           -- full structured judge output (per-dimension
                         --   wentWell/needsWork/missedOpportunities + coverageCaveat
                         --   + notAssessed);
                         --   the *_evidence columns are legacy (pre-8-dim rows)
  deterministic_jsonb    -- math step results (per-step errorClass + source span)
                         --   + data-leak audit
  model_answer_jsonb     -- structure / key math / recommendation exemplars
  scoring_runtime_ms, judge_model, created_at

session_audio          -- OPTIONAL, consent-gated; off by default
  session_id (fk), storage_path, consent_at

analytics_events       -- append-only; feeds §13 metrics
  id, session_id (nullable), user_id (nullable),
  event_type, payload_jsonb, created_at

voice_tts_usage        -- server-only (RLS on, no policies); voice deploy, 18 Oct
  month (text 'YYYY-MM', pk), used (int), updated_at
  -- the monthly Cartesia character ledger, shared by every agent job on every
  --   worker and the local voice scripts; incremented in one statement

voice_session_lines    -- server-only; voice deploy, 18 Oct
  id (identity), session_id (fk), line (jsonb), created_at
  -- a voice session's per-turn records (lib/voice/records.ts), one row per
  --   line of the old voice-live/<date>/<id>.jsonl; scripts/voice-export.ts
  --   writes the file back out
```

**Enums:** `phase` = `INTRO|CLARIFY|STRUCTURE|ANALYSIS|EXHIBIT|BRAINSTORM|RECOMMENDATION|WRAP|SCORING`. `session_status` = `active|completed|abandoned|terminated`. `rating` = `needs_work|meets_bar|strong` (displayed as needs work / adequate / strong). "Not assessed" is not an enum value: it is a `notAssessed` flag in `rubric_jsonb` with a NULL rating column, shown via `ratingLabel`.

**Terminated / abandoned scoring (Rule 18/19):** a `terminated` session (conduct C2-repeat / C3) produces **no score, no report**; a C5-`abandoned` session is excluded from scoring. The score route only proceeds for `status = completed`, so both are safe by construction.

**Candidate-ended sessions (Rule 18A, v4.10).** A stop request or the End button ends the session: `completed` with a report when something assessable happened (`flags.endReason = candidate_stop | candidate_end_button`, `flags.partial` when the case was cut short — no overall rating, the score row's `overall_rating` NULL), else `abandoned` (no report). No new `session_status` value — that would need a migration (flag it if wanted). A C5 offer accepted after the case is finished also completes with the report (`endReason = distress_stop_after_case`).

`content_jsonb` holds the full case schema (§11 of product PRD). It is **loaded server-side only**; the client receives the read-aloud prompt and exhibits-as-shown, never the ledger answers or keys.

---

## 5. The orchestrator — anti-hallucination is the core invariant

This is where FR-4 ("zero hallucinated figures") is won or lost. **The acceptance criterion is hard: 0 invented numbers across a 50-case QA run.** Build for that from line one, not as a later hardening pass.

### 5.1 Data-gating mechanism

1. The interviewer LLM **never receives un-revealed numeric values in its prompt.** The per-turn prompt includes: case prompt, current phase, conversation history, the *labels* of ledger items (so it knows what data exists and can decide whether the candidate's request warrants revealing it), exhibit titles, and the values of items **already in `revealed_data`** — nothing more. Labels, exhibit titles and code's stage lines may carry no figure the case prompt doesn't state (all-case test, 13 Oct — four new-case labels/titles did: "purchase intent at $3.99"); footnotes with figures live in exhibit `notes`, shown only with the exhibit.
2. To disclose a number, the LLM **declares** the request in its turn (`requests`: what was asked, the catalog ids that cover it, explicit ask vs passing mention, release now or later — §5.3). **Code decides and speaks it** (`data-decisions.ts`): only items the case holds and hasn't released, only explicit asks (a passing mention is offered, never released — and only when the candidate states a need for it: "I'd want…", "whether…", "worth checking the actual COGS percentage" — `mentionStatesNeed`), at most three a turn (the rest deferred out loud; a group of items the candidate asked for — a `factAnchors` group named in the model's label or in the candidate's own asking sentence — goes out whole once one of its items does, Lorenzo 15 Oct), the stall rung-3 rescue item only on a rung-3 turn. The value spoken is the ledger's approved wording, rendered by code between the model's `say` and its question; the model never writes a data line, a refusal or a deferral. An item counts as revealed when the segment carrying it is **delivered** (voice: played) — a reveal whose segment was not delivered is not booked. `release_when` stays an authoring/pacing hint: the model's now-or-later decides timing within those bounds (spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-06-plan-owns-decisions-design.md` D2).
3. The system prompt forbids stating any number not provided in context. But the prompt is the soft layer — the hard layer is that **the model literally does not have the un-revealed values**, so it can't leak what it never saw.
4. **Post-turn audit (deterministic, not LLM):** after every interviewer turn, scan the spoken text for numeric tokens and verify each appears in `revealed_data` or is derivable from already-revealed / candidate / orchestrator-derived values. Any unexplained number → flag, log, and in QA mode fail the run. This audit proves the "zero hallucinated data" metric. It is the base of a wider post-turn suite (see §5.4): the tiered numeric-provenance audit (word-number aware), the style/length audit, and the meta-leak strip all run here too.
5. **Orchestrator-initiated disclosure** — all through the same ledger, revealed-only and audited: (a) an exhibit with `coversLedgerItems` marks those items revealed when shown (it displayed them); an exhibit with a release order (`after` — earlier exhibits, or the `brainstorm` stage (Hollis Bay's sampling exhibit; out once asked, or under time pressure) — and `requiresItems`, 13 Oct) goes out by no path — request, covering ask, the model's handover, code's exhibit stage — before its turn (and the brainstorm itself may wait on exhibits, `brainstorm.after`: Larkspur's waits on Exhibit 3 — code hands over the next ready exhibit instead, Settle holds the model's brainstorm move or the case's own ask, nothing counts as the brainstorm before it's due, no wait under time pressure; Lorenzo 13 Oct), nor does an item only it shows (deferred out loud; open asks for it are not forced out each turn); a `required` exhibit still unshown is owed in the analysis and end gates (Lorenzo, 13 Oct): code hands it over in place of a recommendation ask or close, and under time pressure it goes out with the recommendation ask; it also has a deadline (Lorenzo, 15 Oct; batch 26 Blake 4755c03a never got prof-001's): a lead exhibit (no `after`) still unshown when the analysis budget is spent is handed over by code, one ordered after an exhibit or the brainstorm when the exhibit budget is (§5.2 stage deadlines); prof-001's exhibit is required since 15 Oct; (b) before any recommendation ask (code-written time warning / grace ask, or the coverage-complete ask) up to two open requests are released first (Rule 11); (c) a code-written close answers the final message's explicit asks before the goodbye.

### 5.2 State machine

`INTRO → CLARIFY → STRUCTURE → ANALYSIS → EXHIBIT → BRAINSTORM → RECOMMENDATION → WRAP → SCORING`

- **Phase is derived in code** (Rule 8, `progress.ts`): the model declares what its question does (`move`: clarify, structure, pressure_test, analysis, exhibit, brainstorm, risk, recommendation, other) and the phase rises to the highest stage the turn's move and code's own actions imply — releases → their `release_when`, an exhibit → EXHIBIT, a recommendation ask → RECOMMENDATION, a close → SCORING; forward only, never past RECOMMENDATION except by close. INTRO leaves after the first exchange. Stage administration (brainstorm, risk probe, recommendation asks) is read from the recorded moves (`flags.moves`), with the wording regexes only for turns recorded before moves existed. A separate `advancedLastTurn` flag gates the interviewer's *visible* behavior shift. There is no `advance_phase` and no per-phase action legality any more.
- **Turn kinds (Plan decides, spec 2026-10-06 §6):** each candidate message is answered by one of — `model` (the interviewer model); `rec_ask` (coverage complete, recommendation never asked: the model writes its lead-in, code supplies the ask); and four code-written kinds with no model call: `close`, `grace_ask`, `time_warning`, `rung1` (stall rung 1 restates the stored last question). Code-written kinds answer the message's data requests first (synchronous Haiku classification) and still pass the distress gate. Since 9 Oct they answer only what the candidate *asked the interviewer for* (`addressedAskSentences` in `request-signal.ts`, any phase): a sentence with an addressed-ask phrasing ("could I get…", "could you share…", "do we have…", "do we know how many / how much…", "any numbers on…"; "I'd want/need the…" only before the brainstorm or as a question), a question about case data, or a numbered list right after such a sentence. **At the close only**, a question the candidate asks themselves is no ask (no addressed cue, their own statements follow, either/or or self-framed: "Is it transaction count falling, or…? Those need different responses."; batch-24 ruling 4c); on the grace ask, time warning and rung 1 it stays an ask (Lorenzo, 15 Oct). A classifier detection is answered only when such a sentence covers it (a lone data question or an "I'd want" sentence covers only what it names); conditions and next steps in a final answer are not answered (behaviour spec Rule 12 item 6 — accepted trade-off: statement-form needs like Camila's are no longer answered at the close; 9 Oct smoke run, where the goodbye released two figures for conditions).
- **v4.10 additions:** two more code-written kinds — `stage_exhibit` (code hands over the owed exhibit and asks about it) (for a case with `required` exhibits: the next unshown one, one per turn, until all are out — 13 Oct) and `stage_brainstorm` (code asks the owed brainstorm — the case's own `brainstorm.ask`, else a case-neutral generic question; on a turn whose own data line hands over data it asks about the figures instead and the brainstorm waits a turn, 13 Oct) — chosen when the case would close or code would ask for the recommendation while an interviewer-led case still owes the stage, or when the stage's deadline passes (15 Oct, above), not under time pressure, each at most once (Rule 9; `stage-evidence.ts`). A case with `brainstorm.required: false` (Quillpath) owes no brainstorm and its end gate doesn't wait for one; the case's own brainstorm question counts as the brainstorm when the interviewer asks it (`brainstorm.ts`). Rung 1 restates the stored question as written after a colon ("The question on the table: Lay out …"), joined by "is" only for one sentence opening with a question word (13 Oct; batch 21 "is lay out …"). Exit turns (Rule 18A) are scripted plans decided before the model: the end, one clarifying question, the refusal bound's offer to stop; each waits for the distress and conduct screens. While the pressure-test probe is open the stall ladder fires no rung. The close line follows the end reason (`time_up` / `complete` / `unresolved`).
- **Single wall clock, per-phase budgets.** There is one total case budget per case (`pacing.totalMs`, default 20 min; prof-002 Larkspur 25 min since 13 Oct — `caseTotalMs`) (20 min — raised from 5 on 2026-09-15, when a human-paced run showed 5 minutes allows only ~3 typed replies); phases carry per-phase budgets from case config (`pacing.phaseBudgetsMs`, uniform fallback). A pacing nudge injects "advance now if the exit criterion is met" when a phase exceeds its budget or the session falls ≥2 phases behind. **Stage deadlines (Lorenzo, 15 Oct — the core-analysis, brainstorm and recommendation deadlines, Claude's extensions, approved by him the same day; batch 26 Blake 4755c03a reached time-up with the exhibit owed on every turn, no brainstorm and no risk question; `stageDeadlinesMs` in pacing.ts):** the budgets' cumulative ends are deadlines for what's still owed — when the analysis budget is spent (prof-001 11:20), code hands over a required lead exhibit and asks back to the core analysis (`stage_exhibit`, `stage_analysis`); when the exhibit budget is (13:20), an exhibit ordered after another or after the brainstorm and the brainstorm (`stage_brainstorm`); when the brainstorm budget is (16:00), code asks for the recommendation (`rec_ask`), leaving the recommendation and wrap budgets for the recommendation and its risk question (the risk question stays the model's; a recommendation ask past this deadline also asks the risk — Rule 12 below). One stage a turn, in the end gate's order (exhibit, analysis, brainstorm, recommendation), each once; not while the pressure test is open, not under time pressure (the time warning owns the last 90 s), and a deadline yields for one turn when the turn carries a math or misquote line (code's flag, the checker's, a misquote correction). The new cases' recommendation + wrap budgets are 1.5–2 min, so their recommendation deadline leaves about one exchange — ruled (Lorenzo, 15 Oct): at least 3 minutes for recommendation + risk + close before their next wave (§15).
- **Load-shedding (Rule 15):** in the final stretch (keyed to *total* remaining time — robust to the phase machine under-advancing), the prompt carries a directive to stop optional probing and protect the recommendation; entry is logged (`load_shed`) so the judge attributes thin late coverage to time, not the candidate.
- **Ending, time warning, grace ask (Rule 12) — code's, not the model's:** the time-warning turn fires on the first candidate turn inside the warning window (90s in text mode, v4.3; 30s intended for voice) — or, since 15 Oct, on the first turn after which the candidate's next message, at their own pace, would land past time-up: the remaining time is at most the time since their previous message (`timeWarningDue`; the clock at each message is saved as `flags.lastMessageElapsedMs`; batch 26 Blake's exchanges took ~92 s, t23 came 1.4 s before the window and t25 after time-up, so no warning fired) — unless the recommendation is already in. **A late recommendation ask also asks for the biggest risk (Lorenzo, 15 Oct; batch 26 Blake 4755c03a, checker on: the recommendation came only with the time warning and time-up left no turn for the risk question):** the time-warning line and code's recommendation ask past the recommendation deadline (`recAskLate`: a `rec_ask` turn at or past the brainstorm budget's end, whatever opened it; `LATE_RECOMMENDATION_ASKS`) ask both in one question ("We're almost out of time — what would you tell the CEO, and what's the biggest risk to it?"); the stage record counts the recommendation and the risk as asked from that one line, so the case closes on the next reply that gives the recommendation. The grace ask at time-up is not in the ruling and is unchanged. If time runs out and no recommendation ask was ever delivered, the time-up turn is a grace ask and the next message ends the case. The case closes when time is up (grace ask done or not due), or when the end is allowed and either the recommendation is in and the risk probe was asked, or the recommendation was refused twice. Every close is one scripted line. Open data requests are released before any recommendation ask (§5.1).
- **Coverage-gated ending:** a background coverage agent (Haiku, run via `after()`) scores each dimension 0–100 on *evidence sufficiency* every turn; the case can't close on coverage until every dimension is sufficiently tested and the recommendation was asked (or time is up), and the interviewer is steered toward the undertested ones. Keeps the interviewer from wrapping early and leaving weak areas unprobed. Normative detail in `docs/scoring-qa.md`.

### 5.3 The interviewer's turn (declarations + words)

One JSON object per turn, constrained by `output_config.format` and streamed (`lib/agent/models/turn-schema.ts`):
`{ say, move, requests: [{what, item_ids, explicit, respond: release|defer}], exhibit, rescue_item, question }`.
The model writes `say` first (one neutral acknowledgment, streamed while the rest is written), then declares what the candidate asked for and what its question does, then writes `question` (exactly one). Code decides releases, refusals, deferrals, offers, exhibits, phase and ending, and writes those lines (§5.1, §5.2); the data line goes out once `say` and the declarations have closed. Releases are decided before deferrals and offers, so a request partly answered this turn is not also deferred out loud. An id the case doesn't have triggers one regeneration while nothing has been delivered; once `say` is spoken, a request whose ids all fail to resolve is dropped (never turned into a refusal of held data). Field order changed 6 Oct 2026 (batch 10: declarations first put Nikhil's first speech at ~3.3s; batch 11 with `say` first: 1.65s median). Replaced 6 Oct 2026: the `speak` / `reveal_data` / `show_exhibit` / `advance_phase` / `end_case` action list — two authors for every decision produced the batch-9 collisions (spec 2026-10-06 §1).

### 5.4 Behavioral requirements (system prompt + orchestrator)

**Normative source: `docs/interviewer-behavior.md`** (the authored conduct spec, Rules 1–19 across core conduct, data delivery, struggling-candidate handling, and a conduct/wellbeing track). The prompt encodes the rules; **deterministic backstops** in the orchestrator enforce what a prompt can't reliably guarantee. This section summarizes; the doc governs.

Core prompt constraints: neutral affect / never grade mid-case (1); force structure + live math (2); no fabricated candidate claims (3); one candidate task per turn, Socratic not coaching (4); 1–3 sentence spoken turns, no markdown (5); never adopt candidate-derived figures as fact (6) — the model never issues a math correction or states a replacement figure on its own; corrections come only from a valid recompute flag (Rules 2/14, v4.3), and a suspected misquote gets "check that against the figures"; pressure-test the opening structure (7) — a request made before the probe is answered is deferred out loud with its reason, every time, and the open probe is bounded (re-ask, narrowed probe, then a waiver — §5.4, v4.9); reveal data the candidate has earned and asked for; every data request is released, refused, or audibly deferred — never ignored, never substituted (10/11); an explicit ask the model leaves unmapped is not proof the case lacks the data — a narrow alias maps it only when it names one catalog label with nothing beside it but articles and period words the item covers, and an unmapped deferral is never a refusal (v4.9). Withhold-until-asked and jailbreak-resistance (FR-5/FR-8) still hold — and a jailbreak still can't surface un-revealed numbers (§5.1). The prompt states each rule once and carries a PRIORITY block (turn note > time pressure > coverage/pacing/open requests > defaults; numbers and attribution never overridden except by a recompute figure) — behavior doc, Precedence hierarchy, v4.8.

Deterministic backstops (all in `/lib/orchestrator`, logged; QA-gated in the harness):
- **Numeric provenance audit** — every quantity in an interviewer turn must trace to revealed ledger values, a candidate-attributed figure, or an orchestrator-derived value; word-numbers and ranges normalized; unit-bearing unmatched quantities block, bare counts log. **Block withholds** (v4.3): sentences carrying a blocked figure are stripped before the turn is spoken (`enforceNumericProvenance`) — the audit previously only logged, and a persona run delivered unrevealed revenue.
- **Exact lookup of table cells and ledger figures (13 Oct, Lorenzo after Larkspur; `cell-lookup.ts`)** — beside the misquote grammar (F2), every cell of a case's `table` exhibits is a fact (subject = row, quantity = column or row label, period, value, kind, $M or $ scale), out once its exhibit is shown or the ledger line stating it is revealed. A candidate's figure is a misread when its sentence quotes that quantity (named next to the figure, or a "from X to Y" chain off a correct figure of the subject), in the same kind and scale, matches none of its figures (for the subject and period when named) and sits within 10% of the nearest one. Not quotes: changes between periods, the case's math-step answers, computed results ("that's", "="), per-flight/per-passenger rates, derived quantities ("new", "lost", "combined"), hypotheticals in the figure's clause, projections in its sentence. The correction is the ledger line that shows the cell, in its own words (the misquote path). Charts (prof-001's exhibit) stay with the grammar. Validated on Larkspur: `scripts/step-binding-check.ts --misquotes`, labels in `evals/misquote-labels.json`. **Ledger lines no exhibit shows (13 Oct, Larkspur ruling b):** read figure by figure for the quantity named next to each ("variable cost of $11,000 per flight", "the average fare fell from $240 to $160" — the case's first and last table periods, "a 75% load factor", "80 seats", "$20 of extras per passenger"); not figures: ranges, years, sizes ("80-seat"), differences ("30% lower"), changes ("up 40%", "a 10% increase"), bounds ("fewer than 5%"), durations, billions; the subject is a table subject the line names. Out only once that line is released. A rate the line states ("per flight") is matched when the candidate says it; with no rate said, a figure without one is preferred. **Report-only until checked** (`ledgerLookupLive`, no case live): logged as `misquote_report_only` — no correction, no MISQUOTE hint, no echo, no checker dedupe; `step-binding-check.ts --misquotes` lists them as `misquote-report-only`.
- **Recompute + unit-check** — per-turn, deterministically recompute figures the candidate states against the case `math_steps` (respecting `alt_answers`) and detect nested-percentage conversions, injecting a hint so the interviewer probes/corrects. **Source spans** (v4.3, `lib/scoring/math-spans.ts`): a number counts toward a step only near one of the step's `cues`, in the same clause, stating the step's `unit`; a step is checked only once its ledger `inputs` are revealed; prompt-fact steps are `live: false`. The hint never carries the step description (it can hold unrevealed values). A per-step attempt counter drives Rule 14: probe, then supply the figure; case-breaking errors corrected at once under time pressure. **Since 13 Oct (Lorenzo, after Larkspur) no code-written correction of a computed figure comes from a deterministic flag alone:** a step, arithmetic or pricing flag probes, and past the probe (or under time pressure) says nothing; the correction comes only through the math checker — code's own figure for the quantity the checker names, once the checker's figure agrees (3% / 0.5) and the input is bound — named in words with the figure as money is written ("Hub–Lakeport contribution per flight in FY2023 is about $4,200, not $11,429."). A checker claim on a figure code flagged is no longer a duplicate when it carries that agreed correction (code's probe counts as the first attempt; never a second probe); a figure code corrected as a misquote stays a duplicate. The recommendation flag (`rec_math_probe`) is a probe only for these kinds. **The same flag is the bound step plus the claimed value** (Lorenzo, 13 Oct, item 4; Owen c2cdbcc3 escalated on a first mention): the attempt counter and the checker's key are `<step or pricing kind>@<value>` (`attemptKey`, `checkerKey`), so a new wrong figure for a step is a new error, probed. Unit and period lines ("That's 25% of COGS, not 25 points of revenue") stay code's. Before spans, the check produced all three false corrections in the 27–28 Sep persona runs. **Step flags are live only on a validated case** (`stepFlagsLive`, Lorenzo 13 Oct after Larkspur): a case's step binding goes live only at zero false flags on its transcripts; until then its step flags are report-only (logged as `recompute_report_only`; no hint, no attempt, no recommendation flag, no checker dedupe). Only prof-001 is live. **Binding fixes (13 Oct, item 3):** a money step may declare `scale` (`unit` / `million`) and binds no figure at another scale ("$24M" is no per-flight $4,200); `unit: "count"` takes no $ or % figure ("$5 more per passenger"); a number glued to letters ("FY2023") is no figure; a figure the case holds in kind (the prompt, a revealed line, any step's worked figures — written figures only) is not a wrong answer to another step (the new routes' 56 passengers, $9,200, $11,200); nor is the result of correct stated arithmetic in its sentence, or a figure 100× off the step's answer; `flagNotCues` exclude a sentence from mismatches only (a correct figure there still verifies), and a sentence continuing the last one with "plus/less/minus" or an operator shares its exclusions. Stated arithmetic: money mixes scales ("26.4/2,000 = $13,200" holds), a leading minus or "negative" is the result's sign, and the right size with the other sign is unclear. Validation: `scripts/step-binding-check.ts` lists every step and arithmetic flag on a batch's transcripts and the new-case e2e scripts, with hand labels in `evals/step-binding-labels.json`.
- **Style + meta-leak** — 1–3 sentence / no-markdown length audit (data read-outs exempt), and a strip of internal planning that leaks into spoken text ("the candidate has…", "let me pressure…").
- **Stall ladder (Rule 13)** — graduated restate → narrow → directive-rescue for struggling candidates; logged as `intervention` events ("assisted ≠ covered").
- **Vetoes on the model's words** (`vetoReason`, shared by Stream and Settle) — a sentence of `say` or `question` is withheld, never rewritten, for: a fabricated candidate turn, narration, system vocabulary, a copied check-in, an unsourced figure, a doubt probe on a verified figure, a challenge to an assumption about requested data, a goodbye (ending is code's), the model's own data talk in `say` ("I don't have…", "…aren't something I have in this case", "…is available, so I'll give you that"; dropped without stopping the stream) — and in the question, a refusal statement on a turn whose data line refuses (13 Oct, batch 21 Raj t14: refusals were said twice), a supplied recommendation from BRAINSTORM on, a question inside `say`, the "is that the margin problem, or does it include cost that grew with revenue?" probe aimed at a percent-of-revenue claim (`growth-probe.ts`, Lorenzo 15 Oct: only of claims in dollars or as cost growth rates — the probe's own quoted figure decides, points or a share on its base are a ratio claim; since 16 Oct also any growth probe quoting no figure a landed flag covers, `growth_probe_unchecked` — the gate below; quoting none, the candidate's message must size in dollars or state a cost growth rate; the prompt's rule says the same); and in the question, a handover statement ("Here's the cost structure over time.") when another sentence is left — code already announced it ("Here's an exhibit: …"). A withheld question becomes "Go on."
- **Rule 11 data-request tracking** — the turn's data decisions are logged as its `data_request` rows (subtype = the decision) with a `classified` marker, so the background re-classification is skipped; still-unreleased requests are injected into the next prompt as OPEN DATA REQUESTS. On a code-written turn, detections no addressed ask covers are not answered: they are recorded in the `code_written_requests` check (batch scorecard flag `code_written_ask_dropped`) and, when the case goes on (grace ask, time warning, rung 1), logged as `explicit: false` rows — which never become open requests or coverage gaps; the `classified` marker is still written. Ledger membership and revealed state are deterministic.
- **Verified figures + probe withholding (Rule 2 v4.5/v4.6)** — the live check also reports figures the candidate stated *correctly* (`recompute_ok`, with whether the work was shown); a pre-send pass withholds any doubt probe on a verified figure and any explain probe where the work was shown or already asked. prof-001 has verify-only bean steps (confirm, never flag). The unit-check hint is suppressed when the conversion was verified. Batch 2 had five doubt probes on correct math; batch 4 had none. **A stamp needs the claimed quantity (13 Oct; batch 21 stamped four wrong claims correct):** cues under six letters match as words ("rest" ≠ "restore"); a range end ("10–12%") verifies no step; a step whose answer the case prompt gives is a quote unless the step's inputs for one period are stated with it; shown work that mixes periods verifies no step; a flag wins in its sentence (no same-value stamp, no arithmetic stamp); stated arithmetic is `scope: 'arithmetic'` — its hint line (`arithmetic_ok`) and the judge's line ("ARITHMETIC HOLDS") say the operation holds, not that the claim is verified. The probe guard and the interviewer-error marks still treat an arithmetic stamp as verified work (Owen's conceded "58/42 = 1.38" stays protected).
- **One goodbye (Rule 12)** — every close is code-written (one scripted line, after answering the final message's addressed asks — see Turn kinds); a goodbye in the model's words is vetoed. Refusal / deferral / offer lists are spoken with each item's first letter lower-cased unless it looks like an acronym, number or name. A promise (deferral, hold, offer) drops period qualifiers ("…, both years", "over 2 years") and says an item asked for by its catalog label by the item's authored `spoken` name ("the coffee bean price change", "what changed on those two routes"), else a name derived from the label; refusals keep theirs (13 Oct, batch 21 D8). Fallback questions are case-neutral ("Which area would you dig into first?").
- **History shaping** — scripted silence check-ins and pause lines are kept out of the model's history (it copied them); the check-in restates the stored last question.
- **Pressure-test state and request guards (Rule 7, 7–8 Oct; spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-pressure-test-and-request-guards.md`)** — code owns the pressure test: `not_asked → awaiting → satisfied` in `flags.pressureTest`; asked = a delivered question containing a Rule 7 probe (text, early stages; the `move` label is neither needed nor enough) in the model's own words or code's own probe — never a code-written fallback (13 Oct: "Which part of the problem would you dig into first?" had counted; 1 of 166 saved runs with turn files); satisfied = a Haiku 5.5 judge (`probe_judge`, beside the distress check, fails closed; Haiku 4.5 until 8 Oct) says a reply since the question answers it. **"Nothing is missing" (Lorenzo, 19 Oct; voice session 0f1b28ea):** to a what's-missing (MECE) probe, a short reply that says nothing is missing (`saysNothingMissing`: ≤ 40 words, no addition, hedge, "though"/"but" or named gap) counts as answered when the judge didn't count it — no re-ask, never the narrowed "name one thing your structure doesn't cover"; code asks which branch they'd start with, once (`ask_branch`), unless the model already asks where to start or the reply already says where they would; scoring is told (event `pressure_test_nothing_missing`) to judge completeness against the case's structure key and name any gap in the report. A re-ask whose lead names the structure says "it" (no "your structure … your structure"). Until satisfied, releases of data meant after CLARIFY (and exhibits showing it) become deferrals; scoping facts, the rung-3 rescue item and forced releases on rec-ask/grace/time-warning turns are unchanged. After it, pending requests are fulfilled up to the release cap. Bounded fallbacks: a duplicate probe gets one code re-ask, then rotating plain questions; a test the model never asks is asked by code once. **Guard B:** on an explicit data ask (phrasing detector, before the brainstorm) `say` is held and a turn whose requests close empty is written once more. **Judge and structure check:** the judge counts only statements about the structure (data-request lists never count); a structure check (`structure_judge`) gates the code-asked probe — with no structure on the table code asks for one (once), a thin structure offered as the approach counts, and a check timeout asks nothing that turn. **Bound and waiver (behaviour spec v4.9 Rule 7; since 10 Oct, after batch 18 v2 wave 1):** every reply while the probe is open counts, whatever the judge says and whoever writes the turn; only a model turn carries a ladder step — reply 1 one code re-ask, reply 2 a narrowed probe, and on the first model turn at or after reply 3 a **waiver** (state `waived`, not satisfaction; "Let's leave the structure there."; held requests released through the cap, the rest deferred out loud; event `intervention / pressure_test_waived`). Actual maximum: reply 4 when stall rung 1 (once a session) lands on reply 3; the time warning, grace ask and recommendation ask can each add one and release open requests themselves. With no structure ever given: five held turns after code's structure ask → waiver ("Let's move on to the analysis."). Every gate-held ask is answered with its reason each turn; a model question about undelivered figures is replaced and delivery claims in `say` are withheld; the awaiting turn note shows in any stage. The judge reads the probe as heard (original + code's follow-ups); a hedged pick with a reason answers a which-first question, and declining one part of a compound probe doesn't cancel an answer to another. **Scoring** gets a neutral record of a waiver (`summarizePressureTest`): whether the probe was delivered, the questions and replies, and that code closed it — not an assist, not an unanswered question, not by itself a weakness; the never-asked path is reported as not administered.
- **Background math checker (live since batch 23, 13 Oct; `math-checker-live.ts`)** — on each candidate message with a quantitative claim, Plan starts the model check (Sonnet 5.5, `MATH_CHECKER_CONFIG`, "off" switches it off) beside the interviewer call; the turn never awaits it (the runner hands it to `defer` or leaves it running, and writes its `math_checker` verdict row only once the turn is accepted; a cancelled turn aborts it; errors and the 30 s timeout (Lorenzo) are logged — a row and a warning — with no effect). Later turns read landed verdicts: a flag on a claim the deterministic checks flagged in the message (recompute, pricing, unit, arithmetic, or a misquote code corrects) releases code's line and is itself dropped as a duplicate (the gate, next bullet); a checker "correct" on a code-flagged figure drops code's line (logged `disagrees_with_code`); a checker error on a stamped figure drops the stamp (and a word-quantity flag carries no figures — its error is the words); a claim the candidate has since withdrawn ("let me redo", "that's my error"), revised (a sentence opening "Actually," with a figure) — a concession withdraws only the claim it answers: the one the interviewer just questioned (the checker line delivered on that turn, or the model asking about its figures), one whose own figure the message names, or one the message redoes to its right figure (Lorenzo, 14 Oct; batch 24 Owen, Marisol) — a right figure that is also a case figure counts only in a sentence naming the claim's quantity (the checker's `quantity`, less plain words and "dollar"; Lorenzo, 15 Oct; batch 26 Marisol 2456aa9d t7's "Let me redo it in dollars", about COGS, said the revenue growth 15%, her labor-dollars claim's right figure), restated (same pricing claim at the same price; the same math step) or answered with the right figure is stale and dropped. The right figure includes its equivalent forms (Lorenzo, 15 Oct; batch 25): "1+x = 1.237" is 23.7%; for a "needed" price claim, a price stated as the answer ("about a 24% price increase", not a scenario like "a 10% rise recovers…") to the nearest point, even when it is also a case figure; the price for the prompt's prior margin (a redo for another target — Owen's "8-12%" to recover 16 points redone as 24% for the 24% margin); and the right figure on the other period's revenue, said with its base ("18.4 scaled to today's revenue" for 16 points). A figure of the same message built on an answered one (its sum or difference with a figure beside it that isn't itself flagged — Marisol's "6.3" = 16.0 − 9.7) goes with it; a restatement answers nothing. Concessions include "I was loose/sloppy", "my earlier range/number/estimate was wrong/off/understated", "I made the same mistake again". A later "correct" verdict on a restatement of an open flag's result (its first figure or word quantity, the same named quantity) closes it only when code's own figure for the flag's quantity agrees with the checker's (3% or 0.5, the corrections' rule); otherwise it is logged `disagrees_with_code` and the flag stays open for its next attempt (Lorenzo, 15 Oct; Jasmine's "a third"). At most one checker flag a turn, conclusion-changing first, none beside a deterministic flag or a misquote correction; under Rule 14 with the deterministic checks' shared counter (key: the step id, the pricing kind, else the claim's words; a model probe on the claim since counts as the probe; **at most two probes per step per interview, whatever the value** — `flags.stepProbes` counts code's probes and the checker's probe, pointer and hint per step (the key without its value); a capped step gets code's correction if it can write one, else the report, and its flag closes; a word quantity — half, a third, a quarter, a fifth, twice — identifies a restated claim like a figure; Lorenzo, 13 Oct, Larkspur ruling c): first the vetted probe, then code's correction, else the pointer probe, else the hint probe, else unresolved for the report; time pressure corrects a conclusion-changing flag at once and sheds a minor one. It reaches the model as a MATH CHECK FLAG hint on a model turn; on other code-written turns it waits — up to two later messages (Lorenzo), then it expires to the report. **A line counts as delivered only if it is heard (Lorenzo, 15 Oct; batch 26 Jasmine 074b0534 t31: the probe on "about 5 points" was logged as used and the model asked for the recommendation instead):** Settle checks the delivered text holds the vetted line (`lineHeard`: case, quotes, dashes and spacing aside; a paraphrase is not the line — it also skipped the vetting); if the model's question left it out, code's line replaces the question (a correction goes before it — "say" is already spoken); if a pressure-test step or a stage code administers took the question (a stage hold wins), Plan's booking is undone — attempt, handled list, step count — the decision is logged `undelivered`, and the flag stays pending for a later turn (check `checker_line`). **A due stage question wins over a checker probe (Lorenzo, 15 Oct; batch 26 Jordan 6bd9edf3 t19 lost its risk question to "Walk me through that calculation."):** a stage is due at or past its deadline (§5.2: the brainstorm at the exhibit budget's end, the recommendation at the brainstorm budget's end) or after the time warning, the risk question once the recommendation is in — and only while the stage is still outstanding; not merely because the model asked it (Plan's `stagesDue`). When the model's own question asks a due stage (`stageQuestions` in stage-evidence.ts, the stage record's own reading) and leaves the probe out, the question stays and the probe is held back: the booking is undone, the decision logged `undelivered` with the due stage named, and `flags.mathChecker.held` keeps its id — a held line doesn't expire, is raised first on the next turn, and code's coverage-triggered recommendation ask yields that turn to it (as the deadlines yield to a math line). A line is held at most once: if the model again asks a due stage without it, code says the line first, then the stage question. A correction is no probe — it goes first and the stage question follows, the same turn. At the close a held line holds the close one turn whether or not it changes the conclusion ("Before we wrap up — " and the line), at most once an interview (`flags.heldCloseHold`, a counter apart from `recMathProbed`), never at time-up. **The final answers are checked before the close** (Lorenzo, 16 Oct; `close-hold.ts`, `flags.closeHold`): from the recommendation reply (the first message after the recommendation ask the stage record holds) on, a checker flag holds the close whatever its rating — code's "Before we wrap up — " probe, or Rule 14's correction — once per figure (the same key, or its step at the same value; the checker's line heard on a model turn counts); the closing message's own deterministic flag goes first; such a flag is never shed under time pressure nor expired by the two-message wait. The redo — the reply to code's probe (any `rec_math_probe`) — holds nothing and is not waited on. A final answer the checker ran on whose verdict hasn't landed holds the close one turn (`close_wait`: the stage's natural follow-up), never for the follow-up's own answer or the redo, nor twice in a row. **At most two wrap-up probes an interview** (every code "Before we wrap up" probe or correction, `closeHold.probeTurns`; Lorenzo, 16 Oct): the recommendation reply's figures first, then the largest relative error; the rest go to the report, and with none left there is no follow-up. Only time-up overrides it. Before the recommendation the earlier rules stand. The hold acts on the checker's vetted **flag**, not on a bare "error" verdict: an error code's vetting clears (low confidence, within tolerance, a word quantity that holds — "most of the gap" for 8.5 of 18 points) holds nothing. None goes out on a turn where code flags or corrects a misquote (Lorenzo). **The close holds one turn** for an unresolved conclusion-changing flag on a message before the recommendation (code says "Before we wrap up — " and the line; once a case, not when time is up); anything left at the close goes to the report. Scoring adds a MATH CHECKER section (each flag, code's figure when code wrote the correction, its outcome), drops checker-flagged stamps and treats a doubt on a checker-flagged figure as supported. A verdict whose own derivation finds the candidate right ("Candidate's figure is correct.") is no flag (13 Oct, Larkspur). A checker probe asks only about the candidate's own claim, in two steps (Lorenzo, 13 Oct; the two-step rule from his Step-4 rulings): the first probe names only what is in the claim (the sentence of the message the checker's span comes from, and the sentence before it, which "That …" refers back to) plus the target (the prompt's % and $ figures and their changes, so "Does that account for the full 18 points?" may be said). A draft that names a case component (a content word of the case's ledger and exhibit text, less plain words and the prompt's) or a figure outside that is trimmed of its last ask when what is left passes ("…against the 18, and where does overhead sit?" → "…against the 18?"), else replaced by the neutral fallback. The untrimmed draft is kept as the **hint**, Rule 14's second probe, when it names only components and figures the candidate raised in any message; it is asked only at the second attempt, when code can't correct and no pointer passed (the pointer keeps its rule: it carries a revealed figure). No probe carries arithmetic. The neutral fallback quotes their figure only when it is a short figure ("Walk me through how you got to 10–12%."), else "Walk me through that calculation." / "…how you got there." for a word quantity. One error, one count (Lorenzo, 13 Oct): a restated error joins its flag, matched by the candidate's own figures (a registry in the session's `mathChecker` flags). The count is the stored counter or the interviewer turns since the error was first stated that asked about its figures, whichever is higher, so the model's own probes count. After two probes code says its correction once; otherwise the error goes to the report ("Probed twice; not corrected — left for this report."). The flag then closes: the probe guard withholds any interviewer sentence about its figures (`closed_to_report`), and the model is told to move on. Code also writes a correction for a relative change stated as points (`relativeAsPoints`), whatever quantity the checker named — for a revealed share line named in the claim's span, else anywhere in the claim's message (Lorenzo, 15 Oct; Jordan 78c89dba t5), never for the prompt's own figures or a line's own change in points. **A closed error said again in the final recommendation gets one more flag** (Lorenzo, 13 Oct; Jordan 099f33e9 t25): in the reply to a recommendation ask, code matches a closed flag's figures in a unit the candidate said them in (from the session's `math_checker` flag rows: "about 40 points" matches, "inputs ran 40%" does not; the checker's own verdict on that message lands after the close, so it isn't waited for). It goes through the `rec_math_probe` path: code's correction again with "Does that change your recommendation?" when it wrote one, else "Before we wrap up — walk me through how you got to <figure>." (a figure said as "38-point" is said back "38 points") Once a case (shared with the other recommendation math flags), after a misquote correction and a deterministic flag, and not when time is up. The figure must be said in a unit the closed claim was said in — money, %, points — never a bare number (Larkspur: a closed "two separate effects" matched "$2M in year one"). Code-written lines say "an" before a figure spoken with a vowel sound (`articleFor` in `lib/number-words.ts`: an 8%, an 11%, an 18%, an 80%, an 800, an 8,000; a 1%, a 10%, a 100) — the pricing probes (`recompute.ts`) and code's corrections (`codeCorrection`).
- **The checker gate on code's own lines (16 Oct, Lorenzo; batch 29 Marisol 64224460 t15 — "Other inputs rose 11.8 [points]", right, got code's misquote line "To correct one figure: other input costs … are up 37.5%", while the checker rated the message all correct; `gateCodeFindings` in `math-checker-live.ts`).** No code-written correction (a misquote's data line, a unit slip's correction) and no code probe (a deterministic step, arithmetic, pricing or unit flag — its RECOMPUTE hint, or code's "Before we wrap up — …") is said unless the checker also rates that claim wrong: its vetted flag on a claim of the same message, matched by key or by the figure in the claim's value (as the dedupe matches). A misquote's line also needs the checker's right figure to be one the data line states or a change it implies (3% or 0.5), so a flag on another reading releases nothing; a unit line names its quantity and period, so the flag is enough. The message's own verdict never lands within its turn (no in-turn wait — Lorenzo), so code's line goes out a turn later at the earliest, carried in the verdict row (`codeFlags[].finding`) and released by the first Plan after it lands; the attempt counter, the step-probe cap, the doubt licence (`flaggedFigures`, `flaggedThisTurn`) and the deadlines' yield to a math line all count released lines only. A verdict that rates the claim correct (`checker_correct`), doesn't rate it (`not_rated`: no claim on the figure, unverifiable, low confidence, a flag the vetting dropped), or flags it on another reading (`checker_disagrees`) drops the line; so does an answer since (`stale`) or a wait past two messages (`expired`, never in the final answers). Every decision is a `code_gate` event and check; a message the checker doesn't run on (no quantitative claim, or the checker off) gets no code line at all. A misquote the checker rates correct clears its figures from the truth check's echoes. A delayed probe names the figure as the candidate said it ("You said a 10% price rise recovers 5 points of margin. Walk me through how you sized that."; "You put the 25% share of COGS at 25 points — points of what?"). The recommendation reply's lines (`rec_revision`, `rec_math_probe`, the closed-error flag) go out on the turn after the reply, from the reply's released findings; a closed error restated in the reply needs the reply's verdict to flag that figure. At the close, a final answer's code flag holds the close once it is released; the closing message's own findings make it a pending final answer (the follow-up, `close_wait`, as built); at time-up they go to the report. **The growth probe** ("the margin problem, or normal growth?", the model's own sentence from RIGOR) is withheld unless it — or the sentence before it — quotes a figure a landed flag covers (`growth_probe_unchecked`), so never of the message it answers; the prompt says the same. Not gated: the period question (a figure right on the candidate's own base — Lorenzo, 15 Oct), which asserts no error.
- **Corrections on the candidate's own quantity (17 Oct, Lorenzo; session 9ccf0836 T24, batch 30 Noor t16; `own-quantity.ts`, read in `planCheckerFlags` when a correction is chosen).** Code says "not X" only when the candidate stated their figure in code's quantity or unit. What they stated is read from the claim's sentence, the one before it and their next two messages: their figure with a unit attached ("6 points of revenue", "6 revenue points", "$100M" for a dollar step, the step's own noun for a count; a bare "6 points" only where the quantity has no other points reading — a margin gain, a points step without a share base — and never on a share-base step, where it is ambiguous: Lorenzo, 17 Oct), a margin named in the claim's sentence for a pricing claim, a base named for their figure ("10 points of the 37.5 percent increasing COGS", "10% of the increase") or for their units ("in points of COGS, not in points of revenue" — a negated unit counts for neither), or a cost line taken back to a level ("COGS of 58 falls back toward 42"). Four outcomes: (1) **right on their own quantity** (3% / 0.5; a revenue-points step read on the share base's level — prof-001's COGS at 42 for a change or prior step, 58 for a current one — or as a share of that line's increase; a price rise read as current share / target − 1): one code-written conversion line — "In revenue terms, that's about 4.2 points." for a revenue-points step, else code's correction without its "not X" ("From today's 6% margin, recovering 16 points of margin takes about a 20.5% price rise."); (2) **code's quantity or unit stated**: today's correction; (3) **wrong on a base they stated**: the correction on their quantity ("In COGS terms, bean inflation's contribution to the COGS increase is about 10 points, not 15."; "Getting COGS back to 42% of revenue takes about a 38.1% price rise, not 45%."); (4) **no quantity stated**: if their figure is right on a reading code computes (`readingsOf`: code's quantity; for a revenue-points step its share base — points of COGS on the step's base level, a share of the line's increase; for a price rise, a cost line back to its prior level), the conversion as in (1) and the step closes; if it is wrong on every reading, code's figure without "not X" (logged `correct`) and the step stays open for the normal probes (Lorenzo, 17 Oct). A conversion is logged `converted` (with how the figure was framed) and closes the step: its key, the marker `converted:<step>` (a later checker flag on the step is left for the report, `unresolved`, when it restates a converted figure or is right on a reading; a new figure wrong on every reading reopens it — the probe first, "not X" later only with code's unit stated: Lorenzo, 17 Oct), code's deterministic probes on the step (capped), and the candidate's figure (`convertedFigures` in checker memory → `closedFigures`, so the model's own doubts on it are withheld, `closed_to_report` — needed because a figure that is also a case figure, like 9ccf0836's "10" = 25 × 40%, no key keeps). A closed-error restatement never raises a converted key. A model sentence that negates a converted figure ("not 38", "isn't 38%", "rather than 38", "instead of 38") is withheld like an unsupported doubt (`negates_converted`, `vetoReason`, both fields); the checker line then counts as unheard and code says it itself. The report leaves a conversion out of the checker's WRONG list (the figure was right on a reading). The conversion lines stay neutral as written — no "Right," or "That works" (Lorenzo, 17 Oct). A relative change stated as points ("COGS rose 16 points, from 42% to 58% of revenue; 38% is its growth on its own base.") has no "not X" and goes out as written. Unchanged: the unit check's lines (they fire only on a figure said in revenue or margin units — code's unit — and are already worded on the candidate's base) and the misquote line (a quote of a named ledger item). The own-quantity readings exist only where the case has share bases or a pricing model (prof-001); on the new cases a correction is "not X" only with code's unit stated, else code's figure without it.
- **The pointer passes the probes' leads check (18 Oct, Lorenzo; batch 31 Owen 8b041a8f t16: "…the cost structure in Exhibit A, where COGS is 58% of revenue and labor and overhead are largely fixed?" — an assumption stated as an exhibit fact, and leading; `vetCheckerClaims`).** A pointer is withheld when it names a case component or a figure outside the claim's own sentences and the prompt's figures (`probeLeads` against the claim's span, as the first probe), besides the earlier rules (a question, provenance and truth, naming a revealed figure). Its turn then gets the second-attempt hint, or the flag goes to the report. On the corpus every pointer said (5) is withheld — two get the hint, three go to the report (§16).
