- **Voice feel (17 Oct, branch `voice-feel` off `club-beta`; not merged, not deployed; Lorenzo listens first) — awaiting Lorenzo:**
  - **If pointers come back (Lorenzo, 18 Oct):** the strict check (f3a3b06) withholds every pointer the corpus had (5 in 268 runs). If they are wanted again, vet them with an allowlist — revealed figures, the case's line names, question scaffolding; anything else withheld — not a blocklist of phrasings, which fails open on wordings it didn't anticipate.
  - **Voice capacity — found 18 Oct (free check, report only), logged, not fixed:** (1) **TTS budget:** ≈ 2,550 characters a session at batch 31's text pace (2,268–2,775; the full 20-minute ones 2,508 and 2,753), ≈ 3,700–4,000 in voice (9ccf0836: 3,676 heard in 18 min, more and shorter turns; turns cut before audio not counted; the filler clips are cached, not per session) — so the 90k monthly cap (`VOICE_TTS_CHAR_CAP`, less the 2k reserve a turn needs) allows ≈ 22 voice sessions a month, ≈ 35 at text pace (Cartesia Pro is 100k). (2) **Concurrency:** `npm run voice:agent` runs the LiveKit worker in dev mode, where its load threshold is Infinity — it never reports itself full and accepts every dispatched session, each in its own process; the limits are elsewhere: Deepgram's concurrent Flux streams (an apparent one-stream limit on the credit account, above — unverified for two streams, a paid test), Cartesia's concurrency on the plan (not checked) and the machine. A session past a limit: its agent fails to open Flux (or TTS) and never says hello; the browser stays on "Waiting for audio" — no timeout, no message. **Built 18 Oct (voice deploy, brief A5):** a global cap, `VOICE_MAX_CONCURRENT` (default 1, a Vercel variable): the token route counts the other live voice rooms in LiveKit's room list (`case-*` with someone in them, or created in the last 30s) and refuses past the cap with 429 before any dispatch; the browser shows "Voice is busy right now. Try again in a few minutes." — on that refusal, and when no interviewer audio arrives within **30s** of connecting (or of the audio-unlock click; 15s until 19 Oct — a LiveKit Build-plan agent can cold-start 10–20s after idle, which 15s called busy), showing "Connecting to your interviewer…" while it waits, then leaves the room (`lib/voice/capacity.ts`). Two Starts within the same second can both pass (the count is not a lock) — acceptable at pilot traffic; the watchdog catches the loser. The hosted worker runs in start mode (load threshold 0.7 CPU; LiveKit Cloud enforces its own defaults). **For when concurrency goes above 1 (logged 19 Oct, no change):** at each job start the hosted worker's load passes 0.7 for ≈ 2.5s (0.71–0.73 in the three 19 Oct jobs; the job process forking and loading) and it marks itself "at full capacity, marking as unavailable" — a second session dispatched in that window would find no worker on the one replica; raise replicas or start a pre-warmed process before allowing two.
  - **The feel list — logged by Lorenzo (18 Oct), for later, not now:** (1) the stale check misses an acceptance (batch 31 Tyler t10: a misquote correction said again after "58%, not 61%") or a redo (batch 31 Sofia t12, batch 30 Marisol t12/t14) — the same claim corrected or probed twice; (2) Sofia's COGS-split asks never offered the held input price changes (batch 31: "the current split" refused truthfully, twice); (3) the batch-29 Marcus same-item deferral stall (below).
  - **Found 18 Oct; fixed 18 Oct on Lorenzo's go (before the merge) — the live score route didn't wait for a checker verdict in flight:** the harness waits for every background check before scoring (`live-run.ts`), so in batches a verdict that lands after the close (batch 31 Marisol t19 "45%") reaches the report; the app's score route (`app/api/channel/[sessionId]/score/route.ts`) reads the checker rows as they are when the candidate presses Score, so a verdict still running (up to `MATH_CHECKER_TIMEOUT_MS`, 30s) would be missing. Fixed: `lib/scoring/checker-wait.ts` (a check row with `started` and no verdict or error row is pending); the score route waits up to `MATH_CHECKER_TIMEOUT_MS` before scoring and logs `checker_wait_timeout` if it fires; the UI calls `…/finalize` first and shows "Finalizing your report…" (text and voice).
  - **Found 18 Oct (Lorenzo's check on 4a), logged, not fixed — the same item deferred four times is a stall nothing counted:** of the 15 runs in batches 27–30 with two or more deferrals, 14 deferred different items; batch 29 Marcus 51056656 deferred "the cost structure" at t2, t4, t6 and t8 (the model's own `defer` while it pressed for a structure the answer-first persona wouldn't give), and the data went out at t10 when the pressure test was satisfied. The stall check classed every one of his messages as analysis (figures in a derivation), so no rung fired. The variants don't hide it from the logs (`data_decisions.defers` names the item each turn), but they would vary its sound. A possible rule — a same-item deferral on two turns running counts as a no-progress turn for Rule 13 — is not proposed for build yet; awaiting Lorenzo.
  - **Item 4b — ruled by Lorenzo (18 Oct): go on the interviewer prompt only (the checker's prompt shares a call with the verdicts and is not changed); built (`lib/agent/prompts/system.ts`, the "question" rule); one paid batch to measure. The proposal as it stood:** of 391 model questions in batches 27–30, 16% open "With those figures…", 16% "You said…" / "Earlier you said…" / "You mentioned…" / "You named…" (74; 3 or more in 8 of 45 runs, at most 4) — 53 the model's own, 20 the checker's drafted line said verbatim, 1 a RECOMPUTE probe; 9ccf0836 has four. Proposal: one prompt line in the question rules — "Don't open two questions in a row the same way. To come back to what they said, name the point itself ('On the 38% —', 'Your bean figure:') or just ask; 'You said …' at most once every few turns" — and the same instruction to the checker's probe drafts. It changes the model's words, so it needs a paid batch (the usual 10 personas, ≈ $6) to measure the opening mix and that probes still name the figure; kept apart from item 1.
  - **Item 4c — ruled by Lorenzo (18 Oct: "go"; the rate a config value, skipped fillers logged per turn), built (§8.2). The proposal as it stood:** every completed turn got one — 21 of 21 in 9ccf0836 (20 thinking sounds, one "Take your time."), 10 of 10 in 314295e0. The gap is real: the model's first audio came a median 2.76s after end of turn (under 1.5s on 1 of 21), so fewer fillers means more dead air. Proposed rate ≈ 3 in 4, with no latency prediction (his 17 Oct ruling): always after a 40+ word answer and on a data ask; otherwise not on two turns running. On 9ccf0836 that drops 5 of 21 (t4, t8, t13, t16, t29 — short replies right after a filler) → 16 of 21. Free to build; his ear decides.
  - **Ruled by Lorenzo (17 Oct), built:** both first filler sets dropped (the old acks confirm; the carrier cut clipped "so"); thinking sounds in beats; the connector; the say rule on every voice turn; long answers get a filler (§8.2, §16). Demo for his ear: `Case Interview Runs/voice-demos/2026-10-17-fillers-v2/` (every filler; turns 4, 7, 9 re-voiced on their measured timings, with "So," variants of 4 and 7; `timeline.txt`). **Ruled by Lorenzo (17 Oct) on the three points found after v2, built:** (1) the data-ask pool → the one-beat thinking sounds plus "Let me see…"; (2) two beats after answers of 40+ words, one otherwise, no latency prediction; (3) no action — without TTS word timestamps (Cartesia always sends them) the heard cursor would run ahead by the connector's length (~0.3s); accepted.
  - **Found in session 314295e0 (Lorenzo's "not listening" moments) — logged by Lorenzo's decision, low priority, not built (17 Oct; he barely noticed them):** (1) **t5 → t6:** "I don't really think anything is really missing. … I do just need to look at the cost first. If you have the COGS…" — the pressure-test judge ruled the probe unanswered ("say nothing is missing without naming any gap"), though it answers both MECE and priority; code narrowed the probe ("Let me make it concrete: … which one would you check first, and why?") and deferred COGS as premature. Not the stall ladder: the stall check classed the message as analysis, no rung. COGS went out at t8 after he repeated "costs first". (2) **t11 → t12:** "do you know what [the bean share] is now?" got a bare refusal ("I don't have coffee beans' share of COGS today.", no ledger id) — true, but the case holds the related bean price change (`bean_price_change`, +40%) and code didn't offer it. (3) **t9 → t10:** "breakdown of, like, item by item" released one of the three COGS items (`bean_share_of_cogs`): `request_scope` dropped `non_bean_input_change` from the qualified ask and `bean_price_change` wasn't mapped — at odds with the rule in force "a COGS split goes out whole".
  - **Session 9ccf0836 (17 Oct) — a false correction from converting the candidate's figure to another quantity; ruled by Lorenzo (17 Oct) — "not X" only when the candidate used code's quantity or unit, otherwise one conversion line and the step closes; a figure wrong on their own quantity keeps the correction, worded on it — built (§5.4 "Corrections on the candidate's own quantity", §16). The proposal as it stood:** **What happened** (voice turns; DB turns in brackets): seq 11 [T20] the checker's probe on "ten percent of the increase is from the coffee beans" (key `bean_points_added`) — his figure is right on a COGS base (25% × 40% = 10%); seq 13 [T22] the checker's probe, said by code, on "each one is around nine point three" (`non_bean_points_added`; 25% × 37.5% ≈ 9.4 each, right on the same base); seq 15 [T24] **the checker's correction (attempt 2, said by the model verbatim): "Bean inflation's contribution to the COGS increase, in points of revenue, is about 4.2 points, not 10."** — after he had said "ten points of the thirty seven point five percent increasing COGS" (seq 13); the checker read the claim as points of revenue (its `unit`), code's step agreed (4.2), and the gate passed it (the checker is the flagger). Then the model's own probes, licensed by the open flag: seq 16 [T26] "Take beans at 25 percent of COGS … Does ten points carry through to the revenue base?", seq 20 [T28] "Which share of revenue was COGS two years ago…" (after his 5.8 — wrong in the revenue terms he'd been pushed into), seq 22 [T30] "Ten points of COGS convert to revenue using one share of revenue. Which one applies…". **Not the stall ladder:** every stall check that turn range says rung none; T30's doubled "Take your time. Take your time." is the stuck-pool filler plus the model opening its question with the same words (the say rule only reads `say`). 4 more model probes were written for turns his resumed speech cancelled (seq 17–19, 23). **The same class elsewhere:** batch 30 Noor t16 — code's pricing check, "From today's 6% margin, recovering 16 points of margin takes about a 20.5% price rise, not 38%." — her 38% was the rise for COGS to return to 42% of revenue, stated at t13 (58/42 = 1.38), right; she conceded. **Batch 30's hand grading rated this line true; by this class it is a false correction, so batch 30 had one** (the grading file and §16's batch-30 entry say 0 — to correct on Lorenzo's word). Batch 29 Marisol t15 — code's misquote check read "rose 11.8" (points of revenue, right) as a misquote of the 37.5% price change; the checker gate (16 Oct) already drops it. **club-beta behaves the same:** no file on the correction path (math checker, checker line, pricing, misquote, unit check, fact check, settle, stall, prompts) differs between `club-beta` and `voice-feel`; given the same checker verdict the same lines go out. **Proposal:** (1) one guard before any correction — the checker's (`codeCorrection`), code's pricing line, the misquote line: read the candidate's stated quantity from the claim and their next two messages ("of COGS", "points of COGS", "of the increase", "COGS back to 42%"), and compute their figure on that quantity from the ledger (bean share × change; 0.75 × 37.5; 58/42 − 1); if code's figure measures a different quantity and theirs is right on their own (within the 3% / 0.5 tolerance), never say "not X": say once, code-written, "In revenue terms that's about 4.2 points." (Noor: "Measured on margin, the rise is about 20.5%."); (2) close that step: the checker key, the step probes and the figure (`closedFigures`, so the model's own doubts are vetoed as unsupported) — reopened only when the candidate states a wrong figure in code's unit (his 5.8 points of revenue would still get one probe); (3) a figure wrong on their own quantity keeps today's correction, worded on their quantity. Reuses the unit check's own-base machinery (`statedBaseOf`, "right on their own base", 15 Oct). **Risks:** a misread stated quantity silences a true correction (mitigated: their figure must be right on it); a conversion line can still sound like a correction; step quantities need an alternative-base value per case (prof-001's three steps; the new cases' steps don't have them yet). **Replay plan (free):** an adapter that rebuilds 9ccf0836 as a replayable turn file from the DB and the worker log (model outputs, checker verdicts) — the voice agent saves none; then before (club-beta) / after on 9ccf0836, batch 29 Marisol (b29 verdicts on file) and batch 30 Noor (b30 verdicts converted), plus the full 240-run corpus for new false or dropped true corrections.
  - **Also found (17 Oct), built 17 Oct (Lorenzo's brief, §8.2, §16):** the say rule didn't read the question field, so a question that opens with the filler's words ("Take your time.") doubled it (9ccf0836 T30).
  - **(c) Proposed by Claude, not built: the model's first line reacts to the candidate's point, then one single-part question with varied wording.** Prompt (`system.ts` "say"/"question", the acknowledged turn note): "say" becomes one short reaction to what is specific in their point (a stance, a tension or an implication: "So you're reading this as a cost story, not a pricing one."), never a figure of any kind, never a verdict, never data talk, at most ~12 words; "question" asks one thing (no second clause joined by "and"/"or"), and doesn't open the way any of the last three questions did ("With …, what does it / do they tell you…" opened 4 of 9 in session 314295e0; 6 of 9 asked two things, 8 counting "…, and why?"). Voice-only first (the acknowledged-turn note, and the voice flag now built), so the frozen text product is unchanged. The connector already opens the reply with "So, …"; the reaction must read after it. **Risks to the fact gates:** a reaction restating a wrong claim affirms it in words with no figure for F1 to catch (the batch-28 Sofia "5.8 points" pattern, without the number); reactions drift toward verdicts ("Interesting that…") and leads (hints), which the grading veto and Rule 1 only partly catch; the say-after-ack echo rule would drop reactions that read as echoes, so it and the reaction form must be tuned together; varied question wording can slip past F4's undelivered-claim patterns ("Given the numbers you now have…" with nothing delivered) and the pressure-test detection, both built on today's wordings; single-part must exempt code's own questions (the late recommendation ask carries the risk in one question by Lorenzo's 15 Oct rule) and the checker's probes. **Plan:** (1) free — two-part-question, repeated-opener and figure-in-say counters as report-only checks, baselined on batches 27–30 and this session; (2) a paid replay of batch 30's model turns (~200 turns, new prompt vs the saved baseline, same model and settings; ≈ $4.5 at batch 28's $0.022 a turn, cap $6) counting F1/echo vetoes, grading and undelivered-claim vetoes, pressure-test detection, echo-rule drops and the new counters; (3) Lorenzo's voice session(s) and the timing replay; (4) one prof-001 wave (10 personas, checker on, ≈ $7, cap $10), hand-graded against the 16 Oct bar — go only with no new false statement and faults no worse than batch 30. Each paid step on his go.

- Which 2–3 archetypes to author first?
- Audio storage / retention policy specifics (default: don't store).
- Exact per-user case cap during validation.
- drizzle vs. raw Supabase migrations (agent may choose; flag it).
- C5 report discard (behavior doc Rule 17-C5, v4.3): **decided 2026-09-29 — not built for the pilot**; a candidate who continues after a distress disclosure is scored normally with the C5 exchange excluded. Revisit if pilot users ask ("hide from my history" first; true deletion needs a retention decision).
- Live recompute recall: the v4.3 span matcher produced zero false flags across the 13 persona runs, but the corpus held no genuine dollar-impact or per-store errors, so its hit rate on real errors is measured only by unit tests. Confirm in the next persona cycle (Sofia, Jordan, Tyler) before relying on it. **Batches 12–13 (Tyler, Sofia, Jordan run):** recompute never fired — Tyler's misquotes of released data, Jordan's 38-vs-16 point error and Maya's "16% of $480M is $48M" are not case math steps, so none was flagged, and the Rule 14 two-attempt cap never applied (Maya probed six turns, Jordan four). Coverage gap, open.

Replay (180 runs): 6 / 6 lines in 2 runs, both expected — Tobias t17: code asks the brainstorm before the recommendation ask (he never had one; his t9 analysis question had counted); Destiny t37 onward: the model's recommendation ask is held for the brainstorm she never had, and her later turns follow from it. - **14 Oct 2026 — a concession withdraws only the claim it answers (Lorenzo's batch-24 ruling 1; `larkspur-fixes`; free).** Why: batch 24 Owen e64de272 t15 ("You're right, I stated that as fact…" about competitors) dropped his "27–28%" and Marisol 4bf008fd t19 ("Let me redo it." about the bean share) dropped her "27.6%" and four other open claims; both errors reached the recommendation. Built: `isStale` takes each later message with whether the interviewer line before it questioned the claim (the checker's probe, pointer, hint or correction on it, decided for the message before; or the model asking about its figures); a withdrawal or "Actually, …" revision drops the claim only then, or when the message names the claim's own figure, or redoes it to its right figure (a case figure counts there). A claim whose figures are all case figures or its input ("a 16% lift" for 16 points) is identified by its value's figures. Replay (190 runs = 180 + batch 24's 10, with batch 24's 111 verdicts converted): checker off 0 lines; on, 1 line — Owen t15 gets the close hold's probe on "27–28%" instead of the goodbye; Marisol's "27.6%" is probed at t19 (later restated, left for the report at attempt 2) and her "23.2" at t15 (her redo gave 22.1, still wrong); no other run changes (three first drafts of the rule over-kept claims a redo had answered — 1c9184cf "16%", Tyler af62e2e2 "19 points", Marisol "22.1" — fixed by the own-figure and right-figure readings). Mutations 7/7.
- **14 Oct 2026 — a misquote is a figure stated as the item's fact (Lorenzo's batch-24 ruling 2; `larkspur-fixes`; free).** Why: Marcus db0e42c3 t9 heard "To correct one figure: revenue grew 15%" on his correct "I need revenue up about 28% at constant volume" (a requirement), and Tyler 4228be02 t5's "COGS is the story: it goes from 42% to about 54% today, … which is most of the eighteen-point decline" (truly 58%) was never corrected. Built: requirement and target cues (need, have to, must, required, to get, to restore, to recover) join the quote grammar's hypotheticals; in the pronoun chain ("it goes from…") a hypothetical counts only up to the chain's end, "it" is the level item named before it in the sentence else the last sentence's (not an item named after the figure), and a share applied to a base ("It's 25% of 42") is no level. Replay (190 runs): off 3 / on 4 lines, all intended — Tyler b24 t5 and Tyler b20 505297a7 t4 corrected ("COGS is 58% of revenue today…"), Marcus t9's false correction gone, and Tyler t17's own question now keeps its lead-in (its misquote is closed). Two drafts were caught on the replay and fixed: an item named after the figure taking "it" (five false "net profit margin fell from 24% to 6%" corrections), and a share of a base read as a level (Sofia 3baae0b8 t11). Mutations 4/4.

Two causes: the group expansion ran only when the model left a group item unmapped (Raj's model mapped all three, so the ask kept its "split" wording and the scope rule — a split ask isn't about how the parts moved — dropped the two price changes); and "the split of the COGS line" wasn't a group name. Built: a group ask is expanded to one ask per item in its own label also when a mapped group item would fall out of scope under the ask's wording; prof-001's COGS group gains "split of the cogs", "breakdown of the cogs", "cogs line split/breakdown/by", "cogs by component", "what makes up cogs", "makeup of cogs". Replay (190 runs): 73 / 73 lines in 23 runs, every run's first change being a COGS-split ask that now releases the input price changes (the release cap defers the menu prices or one price change with "I'll come back to…"); the rest follow from it. - **14 Oct 2026 — a recommendation the candidate gives counts as received (Lorenzo's batch-24 ruling 4a; `larkspur-fixes`; free).** Why: Marcus db0e42c3 t17 answered "what would you tell the CEO to do?" with "…a pricing problem… So: raise prices, staged…" — no recommendation wording, and the received-reading also needed the RECOMMENDATION phase — so the case ran six more minutes on model questions and the time warning asked for it again. Built: `answersRecommendationAsk` (stall.ts) — the reply to a recommendation ask (code's, or a question the model labelled or worded as one) is the recommendation received when it commits to an action: recommendation wording ("my recommendation", "I'd recommend", "headline:", "bottom line", "the fix is") or a sentence opening on what to do ("Raise…", "Lead with…", "Act on…", "So: raise…"), and isn't a refusal to commit; more brainstorm or a risk answer isn't. A first version took any substantive reply; the replay showed it would count brainstorm answers ("A few things beyond price…") and close without a recommendation, so it was narrowed. Replay (190 runs): 21 / 21 lines in 6 runs, each a real recommendation in reply to an ask after which the case now closes (rec and risk done) — Marcus db0e42c3 (target), 0bfe5f38, Sam 42f3afb5, Omar 22a901a8, Raj 4356f4ca, Jasmine 766f97c6; their later turns fall away. - **15 Oct 2026 — the COGS split is read from the candidate's asking sentence, and the cap never splits a group (Lorenzo's batch-25 ruling 1; `larkspur-fixes`; free).** Why: batch 25 Marcus f8ed869d t7 ("a split of that 58 by category, coffee versus dairy versus food" — labelled "the breakdown of COGS by category", a finer cut, so one item) and Fiona 52743bea t5 ("a split of COGS by component, say beans, dairy, food, packaging" — labelled item by item, two items) got part of the split because the expansion read only the model's label; Jasmine f4fcab5b t5 and Owen d3db78c0 t5 *were* expanded, but the menu prices took a release slot and the cap of three deferred the third item (Owen's deferral line also named a second, overlapping ask: "…the price change of the other inputs and input price changes for beans and other inputs shortly"). Built: `askingSentences` (request-signal.ts: addressed asks, guard B's cues, "I'd want…"); in `turnData` a declared ask mapped into a group one of those sentences names (`groupItemsAsked` on the sentence) gets the group's items still to come (not ones already given: "the rest of the COGS breakdown" isn't answered with the share again); an expansion adds only items no other of the turn's asks holds (the model's order kept); `decideData` takes the turn's asked groups and lets a group that has started complete past the cap (nothing else passes it); prof-001's COGS group gains "inside that cogs", "coffee versus/vs dairy", "beans versus/vs dairy". Tried and dropped: counting a group as one ask against the cap — the replay showed 167 lines in ~80 runs, mostly a fourth item (the menu prices beside the cost-lines group) going out a turn early; expanding in place — it moved the group ahead of the menu-price ask, which was then deferred (Raj 76e4ff6 t9, Owen 57c3889 t5); adding already-given items from a sentence — five re-asks restated the share (0bfe5f38 t9, Sofia 3baae0b t11, …); a dedupe of overlapping group asks — unreachable once expansion skips items another ask holds (0 replay lines), removed. Replay vs 2a47982 (200 runs = 190 + batch 25, batch 25's 105 checker verdicts converted to `replays/2026-10-15/b25-fixes/b25-verdicts.jsonl`): checker off 53 / on 53 lines in 28 runs, all read — every first change a COGS-input item now released with its group instead of "I'll come back to…" (the four batch-25 runs, Marcus/Fiona/Jordan/Owen of earlier batches, 20 more), the next turn's "Coming back to…" release gone or now "As I mentioned:" on the model's re-ask; Eliza 9056707 t5 defers the average ticket instead of two input changes; Jasmine dcf421b t21 gets the owed brainstorm instead of an analysis re-ask (her input data out at t5); four turns only reorder the three items to the model's order. Checker: Fiona's saved t7 residual probed at its "54.3" step (10.5 + 43.3 = 53.8) rather than "35% against what?", since the bean price is now out. Tests +11; mutations 7/7.
- **15 Oct 2026 — a fixed claim isn't probed or corrected again: equivalent forms, redos, more concessions (Lorenzo's batch-25 ruling 2; `larkspur-fixes`; free).** Why: batch 25 Marcus f8ed869d t9 redid his "15-20%" as "1+x = 1.237 — about a 24% price increase" (1.237 wasn't read as 23.7%; 24, the checker's right figure, is also the prompt's margin, so it was ignored) and code still corrected it at t10; Owen d3db78c0 t15 ("I think I was loose… roughly a 24% price increase. So my earlier range was wrong") redid his price for the 24% margin while the flag's figure was 20.5 (recovering 16 points), and the close hold at t16 re-asked the retracted range; Marisol 0f7551c2 t15 restated her inflation and gap on today's revenue ("18.4", her 16 points × 1.15) and the t16 hint re-asked "22.1 against 9.7". Built (`isStale`, `planCheckerFlags`): the factor form "1+x = N"; for a needed-price claim, a price stated as the answer (not a scenario sentence) matching a right figure to the nearest point even when a case figure, or the price for the prompt's prior margin (a redo for another target); the right figure × or ÷ the prompt's revenue growth when its sentence names a base ("scaled", "today's revenue", "index", "times 1.15"…); concessions "I was loose/sloppy", "my earlier range/number/estimate… was wrong/off/understated", "made the same mistake again"; and in a verdict, a flagged figure that is the sum or difference of an answered figure and another figure in the same or previous sentence (not itself flagged) is answered with it. Why the last: with the 9.7 answered, the checker's next pick at t15 was Marisol's "6.3 points" (16.0 − 9.7) — the same redo retracted it. Tried and dropped on the replay: rebasing without a base cue (five coincidental matches, incl. Marisol 4bf008fd's real "14.5" error read as a redo); built-on from restated claims or across the message (Marisol t9's 16.3 read as 22.1 − 5.8; Jordan 78c89dba's "gap 40 vs 18" from labor's 22 — a real error would have left the report). Replay vs fix 1 (200 runs): checker off 0 lines; on 3 lines — Marcus t10's correction gone, Owen t16's close hold replaced by the close (his t15 break-even error still reaches the report: its verdict lands after the close); 9 checker decisions, all read: Marcus 7:2, Owen 13:1, Marisol 11:2 / 13:3 / 13:4 stale (intended); Marisol 21:0 ("explains the entire 18 points", minor, already shed under time pressure) now stale by her t23 "16.0 points in old-dollar terms, 18.4 scaled to today's revenue" — the right figure for that claim, so the report reads "revised" rather than "not raised"; Sofia a1361efd 9:1/9:2 the same outcome with the earlier concession quoted; Owen's 15:3 row absent (closed earlier). Tests +21; mutations 13/13.
- **15 Oct 2026 — the relative-change correction when the line is named anywhere in the message (Lorenzo's batch-25 ruling 3; `larkspur-fixes`; free).** Why: Jordan 78c89dba t5 "COGS goes 42 to 50 to 58 percent of revenue. That's a 16-point move off a 42-point base, so it's grown about 38%… — call it roughly 38 points of margin pressure": the span the checker quoted doesn't say COGS, so `relativeAsPoints` wrote nothing, the flag closed to the report after one probe, and the 38-point premise reached the recommendation; the rec hold then said "Before we wrap up — walk me through how you got to 38-point." (cut from "a 38-point cost shock"). Built: `relativeAsPoints` takes a revealed share line named in the span, else one named anywhere in the claim's message (the correction line unchanged: "COGS rose 16 points, from 42% to 58% of revenue; 38% is its growth on its own base."); `closedErrorRestated` says a points figure back as "N points". Two guards, from the replay: the prompt's own figures are no relative change (Jordan t7's "COGS exceeds 18 points" — the margin decline — had drawn "overhead rose 2 points… 17% is its growth", a false line that existed before this fix), and neither is a line's own change in points (0bfe5f38 t19's correct "16 points of the 18" would have drawn the same overhead line once the message's "overhead" counted). Replay vs fix 2 (200 runs): checker off 0 lines; on 1 line — Jordan's rec hold (t16) now says the correction and "Would that change your recommendation at all?"; Jordan 5:3 is corrected at t8 (attempt 2) instead of left for the report, so 5:4 waits and expires; vetted correction lines: 13 claims gain the COGS line, all Jordan-persona "38/40 points" errors (batch 21 099f33e9, batch 24 5c68b453, batch 25 78c89dba), one false overhead line removed (78c89dba 7:2). Tests +5; mutations 6/6.

Built (`answersRecommendationAsk`): "roll (out)", "implement", "push/put through" join the action leads; a new lead "<lever> first", a dash or colon, then an action ("take" counts only here: "Take revenue of 100 today…" is Owen d3db78c0 t15's math setup). Not counted, as tests: a list of areas in order ("Pricing first since it's the obvious one…", Marisol 0f7551c2 t19's brainstorm reply; "Price first, then procurement, then mix — those are the three areas…"), and the earlier brainstorm-reply cases (still false). - **15 Oct 2026 — the pricing check uses a variable-cost share the candidate states (Lorenzo's batch-25 ruling 5; `larkspur-fixes`; free).** Why: Raj 36b40584 t21 "If variable costs are say 58% COGS plus some variable labor, call it ~65%, then an 11% price rise… I could lose roughly 24% of units before I'm worse off" is right at his 65% (11 / 46 ≈ 23.9%), but `answers.breakeven` used only the case's variable sets (COGS 20.8, COGS + labor 35.5), so the pricing check flagged it for the report while the checker called it correct. Built: `statedContributions` (pricing-check.ts) reads every percentage in a sentence about variable costs except the price change and a figure followed by a price/increase word or "of units/volume/transactions"; each gives a contribution of 100 − share, and its break-even is added to the accepted answers in `checkPricingClaims` and in the checker's `codeComputed` (math-checker.ts, after the case's sets). A correction never names it: `codeCorrection` writes no break-even line for an answer with no case set. Replay vs fix 4 (200 runs): checker off 0 / on 0 delivered lines, 0 checker decisions; `pricing_check` events: Raj t21 flag → pass (the false report flag gone), and two runs that said "42% variable cost" (365538f t15, 7455916 t11) gain a 14.7 alternative with unchanged verdicts. Tests +5; mutations 6/6.
- **15 Oct 2026 — "worth checking the actual COGS percentage" is a stated need: offered (Lorenzo's batch-25 ruling 6, "fix only if small"; `larkspur-fixes`; free).** Why: Blake cd75e075 t15 ended his sizing with "Worth checking the actual COGS percentage rather than my benchmark." The model declared the COGS line as a passing mention (explicit: false, release); code offers a mention only when the candidate states a need (`mentionStatesNeed`), and two things kept it from reading one: "worth checking" wasn't a need cue, and the label's "COGS" was stemmed to "cog" before the four-letter minimum, so the COGS line could never be named as a need. Nothing was released or offered, the model's "With those figures, does your COGS base assumption hold…" was withheld (no figures had gone out), and the fallback "Where does that leave your analysis?" went out; COGS came at t18 after he had given a recommendation without it. Built (small): "worth checking/confirming/verifying/knowing/pulling/getting/a look at" join the need cues; label words are measured before stemming. Now: "You're reaching for the actual COGS percentage. I can share COGS as a percent of revenue if you'd like. Where does that leave your analysis?" Not done (not small — needs a ruling): releasing it outright would make a stated need in a passing mention an explicit ask, against Rule 11's "a mention is offered, never released"; and the fallback after an offer is still the generic phase question (a question built for an offer — "Do you want it before you go on?" — would be a new code-written line). Replay vs fix 5 (200 runs): checker off 1 / on 1 line, Blake t15 only; 0 checker decisions. Tests +3; mutations 3/3.
- **15 Oct 2026 — transaction-volume asks get the average-ticket line (Lorenzo's batch-25 ruling 7; `larkspur-fixes`; free).** Why: Jordan 78c89dba t9 ("transaction volume by store, same-store versus new") and Tyler 47563861 t13 ("transaction volume per store") were refused, and Marisol 0f7551c2 t9 ("transaction volume per store", mapped by the model to the ticket and the store count) heard the store count only — the per-store cut put the ticket out of the ask's scope; the case gives volume through the ticket and revenue. Decided (Lorenzo): add the ticket to `covers`. - **15 Oct 2026 — a later "correct" verdict closes an open flag only if code's own figure agrees (Lorenzo's batch-25 ruling 8; `larkspur-fixes`; free).** Why: Jasmine f4fcab5b's "a 12% rise recovers maybe a third of the gap" was flagged at t17 (pricing gain at 12%; code ≈ 10.1 points) and probed at t20; at t21 she restated it ("~6 points recovered out of 18 — a third… the conservative read") and the checker judged that a correct COGS-only lower bound (6.2), so nothing raised it again and the understated recovery stood in her recommendation. - **15 Oct 2026 — stage deadlines from the phase budgets, prof-001's exhibit required, the time warning at the candidate's pace (Lorenzo's batch-26 ruling 1; `larkspur-fixes`; free).** Why: batch 26 Blake 4755c03a's exhibit was owed on every turn (t1–t25) and never shown — the stage hold administers an owed stage only when the case would close or code would ask for the recommendation, and neither came: coverage never completed, the candidate asked for one line at a time — and time-up (t25, 20:01) went to the grace ask and the close with no brainstorm and no risk question. **Why no time warning:** the warning fires on the first message inside its 90-second window; Blake's exchanges took ~92 s (human pace), so t23 came at 18:28.6, 1.4 s before the window, and t25 after time-up. Built: prof-001's `exhibit-a` is `required`; `timeWarningDue` (pacing.ts) also fires when the remaining time is at most the time since the candidate's previous message (Settle saves `flags.lastMessageElapsedMs`; sessions without it keep the window alone); `stageDeadlinesMs`: the cumulative ends of the ANALYSIS, EXHIBIT and BRAINSTORM budgets (prof-001 11:20, 13:20, 16:00) are deadlines — at the first a required lead exhibit (no `after`) and the core analysis (`analysisCore`), at the second an exhibit ordered after another or after the brainstorm and the brainstorm, at the third the recommendation ask (`rec_ask`); through the existing kinds and order, one a turn, each once. Decided by Claude, beyond the ruling's words, to meet its last line ("the end gate must reach the brainstorm, the recommendation and the risk question before time-up") — for Lorenzo to confirm: the brainstorm and recommendation deadlines, and the core-analysis deadline (without it Blake's t21 became code's "back to the analysis" line and the recommendation waited for the time warning). Guards found on the replay: no deadline while the pressure test is open (Victor 7bcf248 t15: the analysis line replaced the waiver turn); at the analysis deadline a ready required exhibit goes before the analysis line (Larkspur Tyler 3801472 t15: the line preempted Exhibit 2, which carries the analysis data); an exhibit ordered after another or the brainstorm waits for the exhibit budget (Hollis Bay's sampling exhibit would have been pulled forward); the recommendation deadline yields to the time warning under time pressure; a deadline yields for one turn when the turn carries a math or misquote line (batch 24 4bf008f t17's checker probe). Replay vs 64f6577 (210 runs = 200 + batch 26; batch 26's 101 checker verdicts converted to `replays/2026-10-15/b26-fixes/b26-verdicts.jsonl`): checker off 69 / on 68 lines in 31 runs, all read — Blake 4755c03a: t15 the exhibit, t17 the analysis line, t19 the brainstorm, t21 the recommendation ask (his own COGS asks answered with it), t23 the time warning (on the replay his saved t23 isn't a recommendation; live it would be, and the model asks the risk), t25 the close; checker on, his t17 carries the checker's probe, so the stages move a turn later and the recommendation comes with the time warning — the risk doesn't fit (§15). Batch 25 Blake cd75e07: the exhibit at t15 (was 21:xx), brainstorm t17/t19, recommendation t21. Batch 23 Blake ac38cd2: the model's early ask held for the exhibit (t13), then the analysis line and the recommendation ask; downstream lines are the replay's saved candidate messages against new questions. Of the 68 checker-on lines: 16 (16 runs) where the model was itself asking the brainstorm at the deadline turn now say code's brainstorm question (same stage, the case's wording, the model's acknowledgment dropped); 20 replace a model question with the exhibit, the analysis line, the brainstorm or the recommendation ask at a deadline (or the required exhibit's hold, ac38cd2 t13); 32 are downstream — later stage turns moved, and earlier closes where the recommendation and the risk are now in before time-up (c3d664b, 13c1d69, 762173c). Replay limits: on a code-written turn the replay can't see the candidate's asks when the saved turn was the model's (813ebd3 t17's "do we have transaction volume and average ticket?" is answered live by the classifier path, not in the replay), and a saved `rec_ask` replayed as a model turn shows "Go on." Victor (persona 42, who declines to do the case) now hears stage questions where the model offered to end. Tests +28 (`stage-deadlines.test.ts` on Blake's real clock, the new cases' order, the guards; Settle saves the clock); 8 fixtures that showed prof-001's exhibit only in the stage record now show it in the exhibit rows. Mutations 11/11.
- **15 Oct 2026 — a checker line counts as delivered only if it is heard (Lorenzo's batch-26 ruling 2; `larkspur-fixes`; free).** Why: batch 26 Jasmine 074b0534 t31 — the checker's probe on "about 5 points" (a 10% rise is worth ≈ 8.5) was selected, booked (attempt, handled, step count) and logged "checker flag delivered: probe", but the model asked for the final recommendation instead; nothing raised it again and the understated figure went into her recommendation. Jordan 6bd9edf3 t11's correction was logged "delivered" and not said either (harmless — it repeated t8's), and his t9 probe the same. Built: Plan hands Settle the line it booked on a model turn and how to undo the booking (`CheckerLinePlan`); after the pressure-test steps and stage holds, Settle checks the delivered text with `lineHeard` (case, curly quotes, dashes, spacing and sentence punctuation aside; decimals kept); a model question that left the line out gives way to code's line, and a correction goes before the model's question; if a pressure-test step or a stage code administers took the question, the booking is undone and the decision logged `undelivered` (report: "Not raised in the interview (another line took the question)"), so the flag is selected again later. Chosen of the ruling's two options: code says it (the vetted line), and "stays pending" only when another of code's lines owns the question. Tried and dropped on the replay: checking and swapping before the stage holds — it pre-empted a hold that hands over an exhibit (Larkspur Jordan 9ab76a39 t15: Exhibit 3 not shown, and his saved t17, quoting it, drew a false misquote correction on the replay). Replay vs fix 1 (210 runs): checker off 0 / on 42 lines in 22 runs, all read — 8 corrections the model had left out of "say" now said before its question (3 in pre-checker runs whose hints the model never saw live: b284faee t11, f99bb34 t15, 140fb90 t19); 27 probes where the model asked its own question or the replay's "Go on." (Jasmine t31, Jordan 6bd9edf3 t9, Blake 4755c03a t17, Larkspur runs whose verdicts were never live); 5 paraphrases replaced by the vetted line (Marisol 2456aa9d t9 "…and add 14.7", b761f3e t9, 0f7551c t9/t11, 9c6a1fa t15); 2 downstream — 4bf008f t21 now owes the brainstorm whose model ask the t19 probe displaced, and Jordan 6bd9edf3 t21 no longer closes because the model's t19 question bundled its own probe with the risk question (code's line replaces both; live, the risk comes on a later turn). `checker_line` over the replay: code said it 40 times, the model 15, 2 undone for a stage hold (9ab76a39 t15, c2cdbcc3 t17; their lines went out later). - **15 Oct 2026 — the growth probe only of dollar and growth-rate claims (Lorenzo's batch-26 ruling 3; `larkspur-fixes`; free).** Why: batch 26 Marisol 2456aa9d t6 — "Is that what the 16 points on a 42 base actually shows, or does it mix in normal growth with revenue?" against her correct 16/42 comparison of percent-of-revenue figures (a D5, Lorenzo); it led her into a wrong dollars analysis at t7. Percent-of-revenue figures already take revenue growth out; the probe is for dollar sizings. Where the wording comes from: the interviewer's system prompt (RIGOR: "When the candidate sizes a problem or gap in dollars … ask 'Is that the margin problem, or does it include normal growth?'"; also the unused compact prompt); not the checker prompt, not code. Scan of every saved run (365 run files, 45 matching sentences, each read; `replays/2026-10-15/b26-fixes/f3-growth-probe-scan.md`): fired against a correct ratio claim 7 times — Sam a73c92b3 t28, Destiny 15492909 t24, Anika d66b8a31 t10, Marcus f8ed869d t8, Marisol 0f7551c2 t6 (borderline), Blake 4755c03a t4, Marisol 2456aa9d t6; against a wrong ratio claim 9 (the "38 points" family — a unit error the unit check and the checker handle); about the 18 points with no claim 2; dollar or growth-rate claims 26; not this probe 4. Built: the prompt rule now reads "in dollars, or states how fast a cost grew … Never ask it of figures stated as a percent of revenue, or of changes in them (points, or a share's change on its own base)"; and a veto (`growth-probe.ts`, `vetoReason` reason `growth_probe_on_ratio`): a growth-probe sentence (not the candidate's own words asked back, not a plain "You said …" statement) is withheld when the figure it or the sentence before it quotes is points or a share on its base and no dollar amount or cost growth rate; quoting none, when the candidate's message has no dollar amount, no sizing in dollars ("In dollars on my normalized base…", "rose 24.7 dollars") and no cost growth rate outside a share sentence ("COGS is up about 38%" on "a 42% base" is a share's growth); "cost growth" named as a rate counts ("a 27-point gap between cost growth and revenue growth", Jasmine 074b0534 t4). A question left without an ask gets a phase-fitting one; "Walk me through it." left standing is the ask. On the corpus the guard's verdicts match the hand classification on all 45. Caught on the way: the model's "bigger dollar driver" for a points split (Destiny) — only dollar amounts count on the interviewer's side; the bare word "dollars" in a message ("cost per transaction in dollars rather than percentages", Anika); a share's growth read as cost growth (Sofia d8ee5c25); "costs that rose 37 to 40 percent" in a recommendation question (df62341 t39, batch 20) — "costs that rose" needs "simply/just" or "with revenue/anyway". Replay vs fix 2 (210 runs): checker off 15 / on 14 lines, all read — every one a withheld growth probe on a percent-of-revenue claim (5 correct, 8 wrong, 1 no claim), replaced by the rest of the model's question or a phase question (the 15th off, 099f33e9 t15, carries the checker's line on); 0 checker decisions. Not in the replay: runs without turn files (Sam a73c92b3's turn, 2b48227a, 15ad9d8e). Two arms (`MATH_CHECKER_CONFIGS`: Sonnet 5.5 / Opus 5.5, adaptive thinking, effort medium, fallbacks on); labelled set `evals/math-checker-labels.json` (349 messages: 73 recall, 276 precision); `scripts/eval-math-checker.ts --dry` estimates $10.15 (Sonnet) + $23.79 (Opus). Pick order: false flags (near zero) → recall → recommendation-turn latency → cost; Sonnet if both meet the bar with similar recall. **Decided (Lorenzo, 13 Oct): Sonnet 5.5.** ~~A correction is spoken only when code computes the same quantity itself and agrees~~ — superseded the same day: **code writes the correction** from its own figure when it computes the quantity the checker names (a math step that declares its inputs, all revealed; or a pricing answer from `pricingModel` once every cost line is out), naming the quantity and its input ("A 10% price rise lifts margin about 8.5 points, not 5.") — only when the checker's figure agrees with code's (3% or 0.5; else probe only, logged) and the price change or target code computes from is the candidate's: in the claim's span, or in its sentence when exactly one figure there in the input's unit (% for a price or a target margin, points for a target gain) is that input (Lorenzo, 13 Oct); the checker's arithmetic is never spoken, and its output no longer carries a correction sentence or expression; the line passes provenance (their figure must be one they said) and truth like any other. When code computes the quantity, its figure (not the checker's) decides whether the flag stands. When code can't, Rule 14's second attempt is a **pointer probe** the checker drafts (a question naming a revealed figure the claim doesn't account for; provenance + truth; must name a figure from the prompt, a released line or a shown exhibit); still unresolved → the session record for the report (step 3). A flag whose own correct value is within tolerance of the candidate's figure is dropped; word quantities hold within 10 points of share. The checker is given code's pricing answers for the price changes a message names. **Live wiring (decided; built 13 Oct, §5.4; replayed over saved runs, not yet run live):** never waits, not even on the recommendation turn — the verdict lands on a later turn as a RECOMPUTE-style flag under Rule 14 (a model probe on the claim counts as the probe); the close holds one turn for an unresolved conclusion-changing flag; when time is up it goes to the report. Deterministic checks first (a disagreeing stamp is dropped; a disagreeing flag is logged, the flag kept). Evaluation so far: 143 messages + a 37-message rerun after the rulings (§16). **Decided (Lorenzo, 13 Oct):** sentence binding (exactly one matching figure in the input's unit), the 30 s timeout (every timeout logged), the two-message expiry, no checker probe beside a code flag or misquote correction. Landing on human-paced runs' recorded clocks: of 19 replies to a recommendation ask, 14 land before the close is planned, 5 can't (the close answers that message). **Step 4** (10 prof-001 runs, Sofia and Jordan scored; after both branches merge): estimate ≈ $6.4, proposed cap $10 — awaiting his go-ahead; graded by Lorenzo or a separate grader.

- **16 Oct 2026 — the final answers are checked before the close; per-store ticket asks; "which of those two risks" (Lorenzo's rule change after batch 28; `larkspur-fixes`; free; progress.md "Close-hold round").** **Lorenzo's rule (his decision):** a candidate's wrong figure in the recommendation or final answer is report material, not a failure; the failure is the interviewer closing with time left before those answers are checked — batches 23–28 did it in 19 of 60 runs (23 Sofia, Tyler, Blake, Jasmine, Fiona; 24 Jordan, Tyler, Owen; 25 Sofia, Jordan, Jasmine; 26 Jasmine; 27 Raj, Tyler, Blake, Marcus, Jasmine; 28 Sofia, Jasmine), and in 3 (23 Fiona, 25 Jasmine, 28 Jasmine) the check probed the figure, the redo was still wrong, and the case closed. **Built (§5.4, interviewer-behavior.md Rule 14):** (1) `close-hold.ts` — from the recommendation reply on, a checker flag holds the close whatever its conclusion-changing rating, once per figure; a final answer whose verdict hasn't landed holds it one turn (`close_wait`, the stage's natural follow-up); the redo of code's probe closes, still wrong, with the report carrying it; only time-up overrides. (2) A per-store transaction-volume or ticket ask the covers answer whole gets the ticket and not revenue per store (Marcus 9cb2554f t15, Fiona f4bd1da5 t9; ee2c251 only kept a mapped ticket); prof-001's ticket covers "average ticket (per store)", "transaction data (per store)". (3) "which of those two risks worries you more" (Raj 2eb0fdd3 t18 — no question mark, the end gate stayed shut ten minutes), "which one would most undermine…", "how would that change your plan" are the risk; stage-minutes reads the risk as the stage record does — batches 23–28 now owe it only in the two time-up runs. **Tried and dropped on the replay (Claude):** a covers-answer-the-whole-ask rule for any ask (it dropped the ticket from "volume or new stores?" and a Larkspur routes line — narrowed to per-store asks, dropping only the per-store cut); exempting every reply to a hold turn (23 Fiona's risk answer closed unchecked); one probe per step (28 Sofia's "5.8 points" in her recommendation lost to her "4.2 points"); the follow-up once an interview (25 Jasmine's later reply closed unchecked); re-probing a redo still wrong on another step (28 Jasmine "17–18%"). **Proposed, awaiting Lorenzo's ruling:** the closing message's deterministic flag holding the close (28 Sofia's "25 points" blocked the checker's line that turn); the redo = the reply to code's own probe only; the follow-up's bound and wording; the store count kept on a per-store ticket ask. **Evidence:** replay of 230 runs vs 74cfa53 with batch 28's 109 verdicts (`Case Interview Runs/replays/2026-10-16/close-hold/`), the 19 runs extended with neutral replies at their own pace: every wrong final figure the checker recorded is now probed or corrected before the close, except 24 Tyler's "mid-teens" (deduped as a framing of his corrected COGS misquote, an existing rule), 23 Blake's t23 and 25 Jordan's "about 8%" (time-up), and the redo figures the rule sends to the report (23 Fiona "2.5 points", 28 Jasmine "17–18%"); closes move 0–6 min later (three reach time-up). New correction lines 3, all true; fact-check F1 0 → 0; **no new false correction.** Batch 29 estimate ≈ $7 (worst ≈ $8.5), cap $10, not run.

Checker off: all 132 findings get no line. 7 more growth probes withheld, 22 in all. fact-check: F1 0 → 0; F2-next 8 → 53 and D6 41 → 75 — both score the very next interviewer turn, so the one-turn delay counts against them by construction. **Batch 27 Sofia t18:** the checker's 17:3 flag (bound to the two-years-ago step, 10.5) would release a unit line on that claim — the gate matches the claim, not the base — but the line is now on her stated base and names it ("Today, 25% of the 58-point COGS line is about 14.5 points of revenue, not 25."), and no code path writes the bare "closer to X" (Plan-level test on her real turn). - **18 Oct 2026 — voice deploy A1a: the TTS ledger in the database (Lorenzo's brief A; branch `voice-deploy`; free).** Why: a cloud container's disk is wiped on restart, and the old ledger (`.voice-cache/tts-chars-YYYY-MM.json`, read-modify-write) lost increments between concurrent jobs. Built: table `voice_tts_usage` (§4; migration `0009_voice_deploy`, **not run — awaiting Lorenzo's go**), `SharedTtsBudget` (`lib/voice/tts-budget.ts`) over an atomic store (`lib/voice/tts-ledger-store.ts`: one `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE SET used = used + n RETURNING used`). `take()` stays synchronous — it checks the last total plus this job's increments in flight and sends the increment, one at a time — so a segment waits on no round trip; another job's spend is seen on the next increment, so concurrent jobs can overshoot the cap by at most one segment each. A failed write is logged and still counted for the job. The agent and the three paid voice scripts use it; `scripts/voice-tts-seed.ts` carries October's on-disk count into the row once.

The local `Case Interview Runs/voice-live/<date>/<id>.jsonl` is still appended when `VOICE_RECORDS_DIR` is set — `npm run voice:agent` sets it; the hosted worker doesn't. `scripts/voice-export.ts <sessionId>` (free, reads only) writes the jsonl back line for line, plus `<id>.md` (the transcript) and `<id>-db-dump.json` (the shape of the 9ccf0836 dump the replay tools read).

- **18 Oct 2026 — smoke test of the hosted worker (session 9813d6ce): the last word was dropped from the heard count, not cut from the audio (Lorenzo's brief, item 1; `phrase-variants`; free).** **Found:** 10 of 23 spoken replies booked `partial` with no barge-in (the local session 9ccf0836: 4 of 21); every one of the 7 with model text in the worker log lacks exactly its final word ("…which would it be and" — "why?"; "costs?", "next?", "wrong?", "beans?", "dollars?", "on?"). The hosted worker keeps no recording, so Cartesia's word timestamps and the frame times aren't in the logs; settled from the code and a measurement instead. An uncut segment gets `endAt` only after every frame has gone to the sink, and nothing clears the sink without a cut, so the audio was complete. But the idle wait's timer (`setTimeout(fn, end − Date.now())`) can run ~1ms before a fractional `end` (15% of 400 fractional targets on this laptop, 18 Oct; the local session's 19% matches), and then `classify` took the segment as still playing and kept only the words ending 150ms before — the last word. That count is what the heard checks book (`voice_heard` "only what the candidate got is booked": the saved question lost its last word; `held: question` on t1). **Fixed:** `realClock.at` re-arms until `Date.now()` reaches its time (tests: an early host timer doesn't run it; a re-armed timer still cancels; 20 real-clock playouts with fractional lengths all book `played` — the old clock fails 3 of 3 runs). Lorenzo's other smoke-test answers: thinking sounds were off by his toggle (works); the 9:43–12:43 silence was him thinking.

Now "What else could the client do to protect margin?" — still read as the brainstorm by the generic grammar ("what else could …"), so recognition and the stage evidence are unchanged. Rule added to `docs/case-authoring.md` (a brainstorm ask must not presume a topic); a test pins that no prof-001 ask names a lever and each reads as the brainstorm.
- **18 Oct 2026 — the voice export marks skipped fillers; the saved line still holds them (Lorenzo's brief, item 4; `phrase-variants`; free).** Session 9813d6ce ran with thinking sounds off: 22 of 23 fillers were skipped (`ackSkipped.why: fillers_off`), yet the transcript read "Hmm… okay… Is that MECE…". **Built:** `transcriptMd` takes the agent's records, matches each to its turn by the candidate message in order (cancelled records have none), and writes a skipped filler as `~~Hmm… okay…~~ *(filler skipped: fillers_off)*`; a played one is unchanged (`scripts/voice-export.ts` passes the records; checked on 9813d6ce's saved files: 22 marked, t8's played "Take your time." not). **Found, proposed, awaiting Lorenzo's ruling:** the cause is upstream — `playAck` sets `t.ack` before skipping, and `execute` passes it to `runTurn` as `acknowledged` whether or not it plays, so the saved interviewer line opens with an unspoken filler (`heardText`; the db, the scorer's transcript and the model's history all hold it), the turn note tells the model "you have already said 'Hmm… okay…'", and the say rule drops a repeat of it. Proposal: pass `acknowledged` only for a filler that will play — at once for `fillers_off` and `rate` skips (decided at end of turn, before the turn runs); the 500ms delayed skips (`speech_resumed`, `reply_ready`, `cut`) are decided after the turn has started, so they need the runner to read the ack when it saves (a getter) rather than when it plans.
- **18 Oct 2026 — the voice silence check-in (Lorenzo's brief, item 5; `phrase-variants`; free).** Built as §8.5 says, to his spec: 90s (`VOICE_CHECKIN_MS`), one line from a new 5-version pool (`voice_check_in`; his line first, four written for his read in `docs/code-line-variants.md`, held to it by the variant tests), once a silence, reset by the candidate's speech (STT words, or a voiced mic frame the STT didn't transcribe), never while the interviewer thinks or speaks, interruptible, no clock pause, no session end; a record line per check-in. Context: session 9813d6ce had a 3-minute silence (9:43–12:43; he was thinking) with nothing said. **Not built, for his ruling:** the check-in isn't in the export's transcript (it isn't a DB turn) or the model's history; the first silence counts from the end of the case prompt, so a candidate reading it for 90s+ hears the line (9813d6ce's first answer came 1:49 after the prompt). Tests: 7 controller cases (fires once; candidate words reset it; a voiced frame defers it; not during a 100s model wait; cut by talking over it; the record line; off without the setting).
- **18 Oct 2026 — a filler that wasn't played never appears as said (Lorenzo's ruling on item 4's proposal; `phrase-variants`; free).** Built as §8.2 says: `execute` passes `acknowledged` only for a filler not already skipped; the controller's heard report carries `ackHeard` for a filler the turn was told of, and `heardText` (both save paths: `persist`, `commitScripted`) leaves out one that didn't play. - **19 Oct 2026 — b406165f's opener sped up / rubberbanded: agent timing added; job start inventoried (Lorenzo's brief; branch `agent-timing`; free; not deployed).** **Built:** the agent timing in the records (§8.5). **What runs at job start (`lib/voice/livekit-agent.ts` entry), in order, all before `hello` — so before the browser's `ready` and the opener:** the session read (DB), the budget, the case (in memory), `ctx.connect()` (the room), Flux open (Deepgram websocket), the interviewer track published, the voice id and Cartesia connect (websocket), the month's TTS ledger row (DB), the shipped ack clips (files), the session-lines writer, the controller, the session's turns (DB) — each now timed in `jobStart.marksMs`. **What overlaps the opener:** in the job: the mic stream to Flux, the opener's TTS stream, the record writes (DB) — light. **Outside the job, on the same container (found in `@livekit/agents` 1.9.1 `ipc/proc_pool.js`):** start mode keeps min(CPUs, 4) = 2 pre-warmed job processes; `launchJob` takes one and releases its slot (`entry.unlock()`), and the pool loop at once starts a replacement that runs `initialize()` — loading the whole agent under `tsx` (0.6–0.8s warm, 1.7s cold, measured in the image 18 Oct) — while the opener streams. It matches the worker's "at full capacity, marking as unavailable" 2.3–3.6s after each job request (load 0.71–0.73, back under 0.7 2.5s later). `numIdleProcesses: 0` can't turn it off (`numIdleProcesses || default` treats 0 as unset). **Proposed, awaiting Lorenzo (after one instrumented session confirms it):** (1) hold `hello` (so the opener) until the container has settled — the replacement's load done — read from the container's own CPU use (cgroup `cpu.stat`), capped at ~3s inside the browser's 30s connecting wait; (2) make that load smaller: bundle the worker to plain JS at image build (esbuild), so no process transpiles TypeScript at start; (3) the jobStart record line written after the opener, not at `hello`. Tests: underrun counted when a segment's next audio comes after the queued audio ran out (150ms dry → 1 underrun, 150ms), none for steady audio or a new segment after silence, per-turn reset; the opener line and each turn record carry the agent timing.
- **19 Oct 2026 — the sped-up opener: `ready` waits for the end of setup; setup timings (Lorenzo's brief; branch `ready-gate`; free; not deployed).** **Evidence (Lorenzo):** session b406165f — the opener played sped up / rubberbanding and the captions froze then jumped; a Chrome trace shows one 2.86s main-thread block in native browser code (no JS) ≈ 1.1s after the LiveKit signaling socket opened, during the room join / media setup; the turn-1 health line agrees (`longTaskMs` 2245). **Cause:** the page's `hello` handler sent `ready` as soon as the hello arrived, which can be mid-`room.connect`, before `startAudio`, the mic and the level meters; the agent started the opener then, its audio queued during the block and played back fast; `start()` also sent a second `ready` at its end (the agent ignores it). **Built:** the ready gate and the setup timings (§8.5). The mic setup itself is unchanged, by his instruction — that waits for the timings. Tests: the gate (hello before setup, hello after setup, an agent rejoin, blocked audio until the click, a click before any hello, `audio_blocked` then the unlock), the timings (steps and marks from the click, taken once, the first of each kept, a throwing step still timed), the `setup` message's parse. Tests 3,793 → 3,806. Not tried in a live session.
